The build ran on the first-class surface: **one `write` created 6 COMPONENT roots and 12 nodes**, one `edit` bound `clipsContent` + fill + radius variables across all 12, `combine_as_variants` and `component_properties` ran clean (3/3 bindings, 0 errors, 0 re-reads), `get_design_system` was called three times with `namePattern` filters. **All four surviving scripts are attributable to named gaps** — three to [BUG-037]/[BUG-044] (applying a text style whose font the VM lacks, then repairing the style), one to [BUG-033] reaching `edit`'s reorder path — and **none re-implements a tool the agent had loaded**, which is the drift [AGENT-035] and the S54/S57 notes measured. Taken with session 50's counter-data-point and session 55's partial reversal, the entry's diagnosis is now well supported: **the share tracks task shape and named capability gaps, not agent preference** — and the residual 19% is a direct read-out of how many gaps remain open. Close [BUG-037] fix (b) and the [BUG-033] park-and-restore mode and this session goes to ~0%.

- **Reversal, 2nd occurrence (session 61) — and it correlates with [AGENT-026] holding**: Session 61 (2026-09-02, external storybook/"Archer", remote). `run_script` was **22.7% of Figma calls (5 of 22)**, the second-lowest share on this file after session 59's 19.0%, and **all five scripts map to named gaps**: #13 is [BUG-014]; #17/#20/#25 need create-time variable bindings ([TOOL-032]), `setBoundVariableForPaint` on fills and strokes ([BUG-043]), `effects` with a bound colour ([TOOL-039]) and page co-location for `combineAsVariants` ([BUG-036]); #27 needs `fontWeight` binding ([TOOL-037]) plus [BUG-044]'s font-face double-assign. **Zero scripts re-implement a loaded tool.** As in session 59, the session called `get_design_system` first-class in its opening slice (twice, #14 and #15) — **the two lowest script shares on this file are the two sessions that did that**, which is the sharpest evidence yet that the driver [AGENT-026] names is real and controllable. Residual: the set-wrapper configuration tails of #17/#20 (`layoutMode`, `layoutWrap`, `padding*`, `itemSpacing`, `counterAxisSpacing`, `resize`) are all `edit` fields, but splitting them out of an otherwise-forced atomic script would *cost* a call — [AGENT-026]'s cost model does not apply to an assignment that tails a genuinely forced script.


- **Recurred (8th) — new record share, and the first session where the driver splits cleanly in two**: Session 62 (2026-09-02, same "Archer" file, remote) ran **28 of 35 Figma calls (80.0%) through `run_script`, and 11 of 11 write ops (100%)** — surpassing session 44's 68.8% and session 56's 55.9%. **17 of 28 scripts (61%) were diagnostic**, above session 44's 54%. What makes this session diagnostically useful is that the two candidate causes are both present and separable in one transcript: the text-style phase failed on **discovery** (`create_styles`/`update_styles` were never ToolSearched — [AGENT-036], [TOOL-050]) while the variables phase failed on **preference** (`update_variables`/`create_variables` *were* in context and still lost to a script — [TOOL-052]). Prior recurrences have argued task shape as the driver; this one shows task shape is downstream of two independent, separately-fixable mechanisms. Two further genuine gaps account for 6 more scripts: no absence criterion on `grep` ([TOOL-051], 4 scripts) and no scoped override audit ([TOOL-054], 3 scripts, one shared with the former).

### [BUG-024] `set_focus`/`set_selections` report success with `undefined` name and id on remote — [#124](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/124)
- **Status**: implemented — `tools/scan.ts` now formats both results through exported builders (`buildFocusResult` / `buildSelectionsResult`). A result carrying the remote short-circuit's `note` surfaces it plus a shared `NO_OP_FIX` remedy instead of the absent name/id, so neither the `"undefined"` string nor the `.map()` `TypeError` is reachable. A result carrying neither a note nor node details is flagged `isError: true` rather than rendered (the malformed-envelope case, same shape as [BUG-027]). `buildSelectionsResult` also surfaces the plugin's `notFoundIds`, which the old formatter dropped — a partial selection no longer reads as a complete one. Covered by `tests/no-op-verdicts.test.ts`.
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: plugin-bug
- **First seen**: Session 45 (2026-08-19, external vip-workflows, remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 45, 48, 52
- **Estimated savings**: ~1 call + removes a confidently-wrong success message
- **Description**: On the remote transport `set_focus({nodeId:"2329:2"})` (#99) returned `Focused on node "undefined" (ID: undefined)`. **Root cause pinned**: `remote/transport.ts:30–34` short-circuits `set_focus` and `set_selections` before dispatch and returns `{success: true, note: "<cmd> is a no-op on the remote transport (headless — no viewport or live selection)."}`. The MCP handler at `tools/scan.ts:144–152` then formats `` `Focused on node "${typedResult.name}" (ID: ${typedResult.id})` `` — fields the short-circuit never populates. The correct explanation is already written and is then discarded. The agent had called `set_focus` while trying to fix the official MCP's 1×1 PNGs (see [BUG-016]); the message told it nothing and the next screenshot was 1×1 again.
- **Proposed fix**: in `tools/scan.ts`, return `typedResult.note` when present (and prefer it over the name/id format), or have the handler detect the missing fields. One-line class of fix; same family as [BUG-008] (a no-op should not read as a completed action).
- **Note**: result-formatting fix — not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist; no auto-plan generated.
- **Recurred (2nd) — and the false success now has a measured cost**: Session 48 (2026-08-25, external site-foundry, remote). `set_focus({nodeId:"4:2"})` (#60) returned `Focused on node "undefined" (ID: undefined)`. The agent had called it **specifically to clear the `set_selection` page-mismatch from [BUG-018]** at #59 — a sound hypothesis. Because the message read as a completed action, it retried `import_library_components` at #61, which failed identically. Had `remote/transport.ts:30-34`'s own note reached the caller ("`set_focus` is a no-op on the remote transport — headless, no viewport or live selection"), that retry would not have happened. **Escalating P2 -> P1**: the confidently-wrong success is no longer cosmetic — it compounds [BUG-018] and costs a wasted batch retry. Fix unchanged: return `typedResult.note` when present in `tools/scan.ts:144-152`.
- **Recurred (3rd)**: Session 52 (2026-08-27, external site-foundry, remote). `set_focus({nodeId:"149:323"})` (#179) returned `Focused on node "undefined" (ID: undefined)`. Cost was one call this time — the agent did not build on the false success — but the entry is now three sessions old against a **one-line** fix (`tools/scan.ts:144-152`: return `typedResult.note` when present). Cheapest open item in the tracker; ship it with the [BUG-018] page-context change, which touches the same remote no-op surface.


### [BUG-025] `write`'s `componentKey` path throws Figma's raw error with no stated fix — [#125](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/125)
- **Status**: implemented — the bare `figma.importComponentByKeyAsync` call is gone; all three call sites (`create.js`'s INSTANCE branch, `createComponentInstance`, `importLibraryComponent`) route through `importComponentByKeyOrFail` in `helpers.js`, which turns both failure shapes — a thrown import and an import that resolves to a non-COMPONENT — into `fail(message, fix)`. The remedy is the one the tools support: a COMPONENT_SET key is **not** importable (nothing here calls `importComponentSetByKeyAsync`), so it names `get_component_variants` / `search_library_components` and a variant's own key. Covered by `tests/no-op-verdicts.test.ts`.
- **Priority**: P2
- **Category**: plugin-bug
- **First seen**: Session 45 (2026-08-19, external vip-workflows, remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 45
- **Estimated savings**: ~1 call per library-composed build; closes a no-error-without-a-fix hole
- **Description**: Call #102 passed `componentKey: "d09fc85b3553df47f1061ebe97e890e7eeced48d"` inside a `write` tree and got `Error creating node(s): Error: Component with key "d09fc85b…" not found` — **no `Fix:` clause**. The key was **not invented**: the agent harvested it from its own script #58 thirteen minutes earlier as the `Tab` variant `State=Selected, Orientation=Horizontal`, read off `importComponentSetByKeyAsync(setKey).children[].key` in the same file and session; `Button` variant keys harvested by the identical mechanism imported fine (#94, #103). **Root cause**: `src/figma_plugin/src/commands/create.js:161` calls `await figma.importComponentByKeyAsync(spec.componentKey)` bare — no try/catch, no `fail(message, fix)` — while every sibling branch in the same `INSTANCE` block does use `fail()` with a stated fix (`:149` component node not found, `:154` node is not a COMPONENT, `:164` neither id nor key supplied). Figma's raw message propagates unmodified, so the agent gets no guidance on the real distinction: a variant of a published COMPONENT_SET is not necessarily independently importable by key.
- **Proposed fix**: wrap `create.js:161` in try/catch → `fail('Component with key "…" not found', 'verify the key with search_library_components, or — if this is one variant of a COMPONENT_SET — import the set and instantiate the variant, since set members are not always independently importable by key')`.
- **Agent recovery**: clean — one retry (#103) with the Tab instance dropped, 37 nodes created. The `(atomic: no changes were applied; safe to retry)` note worked as designed and saved a cleanup pass.
- **Note**: error-message fix — not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist; no auto-plan generated. Related: [BUG-008].

### [BUG-026] `run_script` mode-mismatch rejection is reported as `is_error: false` — [#126](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/126)
- **Status**: implemented — the rejection moved into an exported `buildModeMismatchResult()` carrying `isError: true`, and the handler's catch-all was flagged too. The requested audit of the other tool files found one more instance of the same shape in the same handler — the plugin-transport refusal (`PLUGIN_TRANSPORT_REFUSAL`), whose prose also misses every `ERROR_TEXT_PREFIX` sentinel — now flagged as well; no other unflagged refusal remains in `tools/*.ts`. Covered by `tests/no-op-verdicts.test.ts`, which also asserts the rejection text is NOT caught by `looksLikeError`, so the explicit flag stays load-bearing.
- **Priority**: P2
- **Category**: plugin-bug
- **First seen**: Session 45 (2026-08-19, external vip-workflows, remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 45
- **Estimated savings**: prevents mis-branching on a call that never executed
- **Description**: Call #52 returned `This script calls createFrame but mode is 'read'; rerun with mode: 'write'.` with **`is_error: false`**. The pre-check itself is good — it caught a real mistake before dispatch and stated the fix — but `tools/script.ts:147–155` returns the rejection **without** `isError: true`, and its text does not match `ERROR_TEXT_PREFIX` (`instance.ts:84–85`, anchored on `Error[:\s]|Failed to|Could not|Unable to|(Read|Write) operation "|…`). A script that never ran is therefore logged and surfaced as a success. Exactly the class [BUG-008] was opened for; CLAUDE.md already promises validation rejections are flagged.
- **Proposed fix**: add `isError: true` to the returned object at `tools/script.ts:147–155`. One line. Audit the other tool files for the same shape while in there.
- **Note**: one-line result-flagging fix in the same family as [BUG-008] (issue #60), but result-serialization is not one of the Phase 6 auto-fix patterns (sync-to-async / type-coercion / missing-batch-tool) — no auto-plan generated. Cheapest item in the tracker; bundle it with the [BUG-016] `export.ts` `isError` work, which is the same change in an adjacent file.

### [AGENT-026] `run_script` used for property assignment `edit` already supports
- **Status**: identified
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: measurement entry; drivers are AGENT-036 (discovery) and TOOL-052 (payload shape). No further action beyond those. Recorded on [#127](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/127).
- **Priority**: P2
- **Category**: agent-behavior
- **Auto-fixable**: no (measurement entry)
- **First seen**: Session 45 (2026-08-19, external vip-workflows, remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 45, 53, 55, 57, 59 (**held**), 61 (**held**)
- **Estimated savings**: ~4 calls per build session, plus the pre-checks a script write forfeits
- **Description**: Script #164 sets nine properties on one node — `layoutMode`, `layoutWrap`, `counterAxisAlignItems`, `itemSpacing`, `counterAxisSpacing`, `layoutSizingHorizontal`, `resize(w,h)`, `layoutSizingVertical`, `clipsContent` — and **every one is already a field on `edit`**: `layoutWrap` (`tools/apply.ts:115`), `counterAxisSpacing` (`:125`), `counterAxisAlignItems` (`:121`), `layoutSizingHorizontal` (`:122`), `width` (`:88`), `clipsContent` (`:84`). Scripts #18, #21 and #29 likewise set `clipsContent`/`itemSpacing`/`fills` by hand inside larger builds. Notably `clipsContent` is the **already-shipped half of [TOOL-027]** — the agent did not know it had landed. The cost is not just the call: script writes forfeit `edit`'s boundary pre-checks, and script #153 died on `FILL can only be set on children of auto-layout frames` — precisely the error [TOOL-016]'s pre-check rejects before mutating — costing a 4,721-char retry (#154).
- **Proposed fix**: agent-behavior — when a script body is pure property assignment on existing nodes, use `edit`. Add to `figma-guidelines` beside the "batch over singles" guidance, and note that `edit`'s field list has grown (`clipsContent`, `layoutWrap`, `counterAxisSpacing` are all current). Tool-side complement: when `run_script` succeeds with a body that only assigns fields `edit` exposes, the response could name `edit` as the cheaper path.
- **Note**: agent-behavior — not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist; no auto-plan generated. Related: [AGENT-025], [TOOL-027], [TOOL-016].


- **Recurred — a new and more tractable trigger: deferred-tool discovery order**: Session 53 (2026-09-01, external storybook, remote). Calls #54-#57 created 12 text styles bound to font primitives via `run_script`, including a failed round (#55) and a repair round (#56). `create_styles` does exactly this and documents it at `tokens.ts:649-662` (`variables: { fontSize, fontFamily, fontStyle, lineHeight, letterSpacing }`). The agent ToolSearched `create_styles`/`update_styles` at **#58 — after the styles were already built** — and then never called them. **The cause is not tool ignorance but search order**: the opening `ToolSearch` (#2) named 12 tools (`use_file, read, write, edit, grep, get_design_system, screenshot, get_local_components, combine_as_variants, component_properties, lint, set_focus`) and omitted the entire `tokens` domain; `run_script` was loaded at #17 and became the default. In a deferred-tool project the agent builds with whatever slice it guessed in minute 1, and anything omitted effectively does not exist until something forces a second search. **Proposed fix (agent-side)**: search the tool domain you are about to work in, not the one you started in — before reaching for `run_script` on a styles/variables task, ToolSearch the `tokens` domain. Related: [AGENT-025] (the monoculture this produces).
- **Recurred, but small and mostly forced**: Session 55 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote). Property assignment that `edit` supports appeared inside `run_script` #57/#59/#60 (setting `x`/`y`/`width` on four top-level sets) — though those loops read each node's measured `height` to compute the next `y`, which `edit` genuinely cannot do. The clearer instances are `strokeAlign` and `description` ([TOOL-041]), which `edit` cannot express at all. Session 55's overall trend runs the other way — see [AGENT-025].

- **Recurred (3rd) — the opening `ToolSearch` slice is being carried forward between sessions**: Session 57 (2026-09-01, same file). #10 selected twelve tools by name — `use_file, read, run_script, write, edit, combine_as_variants, component_properties, screenshot, set_multiple_annotations, grep, lint, get_local_components` — and **`get_design_system` is not among them**, so #15 and #19 hand-wrote `getLocalVariablesAsync()` filters returning `{id, name, type, scopes, values}`, which is `get_design_system({namePattern, includeScopes: true})` verbatim. This is the **third of five sessions on this file** where the tokens domain was absent from the opening slice and paid for in scripts: S53 (`create_styles` searched at #58, after the styles were built, then never called), S54 (five scripts re-implementing `get_design_system`, which was never called once), S57. The slice differs only slightly across the three, which points at a template being reused rather than a fresh judgement each time. **Sharpened guidance**: choose the slice from the task's domains, not from the last session's slice — a token-bound component mirror needs `tokens` (`get_design_system`, `create_variables`, `create_styles`) before the first script is written.


- **HELD (4th observation, first time the guidance worked)**: Session 59 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote). The opening `ToolSearch` (#4) selected ten tools **including `get_design_system`** — the omission this entry has flagged in S53, S54 and S57 — and it was then called three times (#8 `namePattern` for `avatar/*` + the two colour tokens, #10 `color/neutral/*`, #11 `styleType: "texts"`), with **zero** scripts re-implementing `getLocalVariablesAsync()` or a text-style dump. This is the first session on this file where the tokens domain was in the first slice and the scripts that would have replaced it never appeared. The residual cost is small and honest: a second `ToolSearch` (#12) for `run_script`/`lint`/`set_multiple_annotations` once the task revealed them, which is the correct shape — search the domain you are about to enter, rather than guessing the whole session in minute one. **Sharpened guidance stands and now has a positive control**; #10 is the one avoidable call (its `^color/neutral/(300|400|500)$` pattern could have ridden #8's alternation).

- **Held (6th, session 61)** — `get_design_system` called twice as a first-class tool in the opening slice (#14 variables filtered by a 300-char `namePattern`, #15 text styles), zero scripts re-implementing it, and the session posted the second-lowest `run_script` share on this file. Two data points now tie the opening-slice choice to the script share: sessions 59 and 61 both called `get_design_system` early and both landed under 23%; sessions 56, 57 and 60 did not and all landed above 52%.

### [BUG-027] `read` renders an empty transport result as a successful, empty document
- **Status**: identified
- **Priority**: P0
- **Category**: plugin-bug
- **First seen**: Session 46 (2026-08-24, external vip-workflows, remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 46, 51
- **Estimated savings**: ~1 call per occurrence, plus the audit blind spot it creates
- **Description**: Session 46 call #6, `read({nodeId:"2285:365", detail:"layout", depth:5})`, returned `meta: {detail, nodeCount: 0, tokenEstimate: 0, depth: 5}`, `defs` all empty, `nodes: []` — with **`is_error: false`**. The node exists and is visible: call #5, one call earlier, listed `2285:365` ("States", a FRAME with seven state children) in the board tree. **The `meta` block is missing `nodeId`, `name` and `type`**, which `tools/document.ts:87–90` copies unconditionally from `raw.rootId`/`rootName`/`rootType` — so all three were `undefined`, i.e. the handler received an **empty object** and rendered it faithfully (`raw.nodeCount ?? 0` → 0, `raw.rawTree ?? []` → `[]`). `getNodeTree` cannot produce this itself: it throws `Node not found` on a missing root (`document.js:427–429`) and increments `nodeCount` *before* any visibility/type filter runs (`document.js:440`), so `nodeCount: 0` proves the traversal never executed. **This is the same silent-empty-result condition as [BUG-016]**, on a different tool — and strictly worse, because `read` has no guard at all: the screenshot path at least tells the agent something failed, while `read` reports success. The agent accepted `nodes: []`, moved on to sibling nodes, and never inspected the "States" wrapper during the audit.
- **Proposed fix**: in `buildFsgn` (`tools/document.ts:78–92`), treat a `raw` with no `rootId` as a transport failure — return a fix-stating text block with `isError: true` naming the requested nodeId and suggesting a retry — instead of serializing an empty document. Mirror the `hasImageData` guard `export.ts` already has. Audit the remaining remote result-builders for the same hole: [BUG-016]'s fix closed it for `export.ts` and left it open everywhere else.
- **Note**: result-serialization guard, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist (sync-to-async / type-coercion / description-only / lint-scope-filter / boundary-guard / assertion) — no auto-plan generated. Related: [BUG-016] (same condition, guarded), [BUG-008] (a malformed result should surface as a clean error), [BUG-026] (`is_error: false` on a real failure).
- **Root cause pinned — same single line as [BUG-016]** (session 47, 2026-08-25): `remote/client.ts:110-114` returns the **raw response text** when `JSON.parse` fails, so `buildFsgn` receives a `string`. `raw.rootId`/`rootName`/`rootType` -> `undefined` (hence the missing `meta.nodeId`/`name`/`type`), `raw.nodeCount ?? 0` -> `0`, `raw.rawTree ?? []` -> `[]`, `raw.collectedComponents ?? {}` -> `{}` — the S46 output reproduced field for field. The `buildFsgn` guard proposed above is still worth adding as defence in depth, but **the repair is the transport fix in [BUG-016] v5**; do them together. Priority stays P0: unlike `screenshot`, `read` reports success.
- **Recurred (2nd) — twice in one session, and now co-located with [BUG-016] on the same nodes**: Session 51 (2026-08-26, external site-foundry, remote). Two silent empties, both `is_error: false`, both missing `meta.nodeId`/`name`/`type`: **#10** `read({nodeId:"43:22", detail:"layout", depth:4})` and **#18** `read({nodeId:"66:719", detail:"full", depth:4})`. Both nodes were read successfully moments later at a smaller payload — #12 `read(43:22, structure, d3)` returned **40 nodes**, #19 `read(66:719, layout, d1)` returned the COMPONENT_SET with its variant axes. So the node is fine; the *response size* is the variable. Critically, `43:22` and `66:719` are also the two nodes whose `screenshot` failed in the same run — the first behavioural proof that [BUG-016] and [BUG-027] are one defect, matching the code-level root cause session 47 pinned at `remote/client.ts:110-114`.
- **Why this half stays P0 while the screenshot half is merely loud**: the agent recovered here only because it happened to retry with different parameters. `nodes: []` with `is_error: false` is indistinguishable from "this node has no children" — an agent that trusted the first answer would have concluded `site-foundry__template-picker` (a 40-node subtree) was empty and designed against nothing. `screenshot` at least announces a failure. Neither of the two silent empties appears in the session's error count, so the reported Figmagent error rate (30.0%) understates the true failure rate (36.7%).

### [BUG-028] `read` reports absolute coordinates, `edit` writes parent-local ones — neither says which
- **Status**: identified
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: missing-tool
- **First seen**: Session 46 (2026-08-24, external vip-workflows, remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 46
- **Estimated savings**: ~2 calls per geometry-editing session, plus a class of silent breakage
- **Description**: The agent flagged its own bug at 22:55:39: *"Coordinate bug — I offset by absolute page position instead of local. Fixing."* `run_script` #15 (call 94) measured a vector track in absolute space (`c.absoluteTransform[1][2] - track.absoluteTransform[1][2]`) then wrote back as `n.x = trackLeft + x` where `trackLeft` was the track's absolute page X (~2656) — landing all twelve vectors ~2656px to the right of the board. Recovery took two more scripts (#16 re-measure, #17 rewrite in local coords). **Root cause is an unmarked asymmetry across the whole surface**: `read` emits `absoluteBoundingBox.x/y` as bare `x`/`y` (`document.js:222–227`), while `edit`'s schema says only *"New X position (moves the node; does NOT change parent)"* (`tools/apply.ts:56–57`) and the setter is `node.x = …` (`apply.js:533–534`) — parent-local. Neither side states its frame of reference, so an agent that reads coordinates and writes them back is silently wrong whenever the parent is not at the page origin. The failure is invisible to every existing guard (no error, no warning, no assertion) — only a screenshot catches it.
- **Proposed fix**: (a) state the frame of reference in both tool descriptions — `read`'s `x`/`y` are **absolute (page)** coordinates, `edit`'s `x`/`y` are **parent-local**; (b) add a `fig.localPoint(node, ancestor)` helper to the `run_script` stdlib (`src/figma_plugin/src/remote_entries/stdlib.js`) so scripts measuring with `absoluteTransform` have a supported way back to local space. Optionally (c) have `read` emit `x`/`y` as local and `absX`/`absY` as absolute, but that is a wire-format change — descriptions plus the stdlib helper are the cheap fix.
- **Note**: description + stdlib addition, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [TOOL-027] (`layoutPositioning: ABSOLUTE` also unexposed), [TOOL-025].

### [AGENT-027] Frame resize rescales children with SCALE constraints
- **Status**: identified
- **Priority**: P2
- **Category**: agent-behavior
- **First seen**: Session 46 (2026-08-24, external vip-workflows, remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 46
- **Estimated savings**: ~1–2 calls per hand-drawn-geometry session
- **Description**: `run_script` #15 called `track.resize(rail.width, rail.height)` on a frame whose twelve child VECTORs carried `constraints: SCALE`. Figma stretched them ~19%, which the agent detected only after a screenshot round trip and then had to undo (22:54:59: *"The track resize had also scaled the vectors 19% (SCALE constraints). Restoring their true geometry…"*). Figma-native behavior, correctly diagnosed, but not documented anywhere in the agent-facing surface.
- **Proposed fix**: add to the `figma-guidelines` skill: resizing a frame rescales children whose `constraints` are `SCALE`; read `constraints` before resizing a container that holds hand-authored geometry, or restore explicit geometry after the resize. Pair with [BUG-028]'s coordinate note — both are `run_script` geometry-editing footguns.


### [TOOL-031] `run_script` is stateless per call — no supported way to reuse a preamble
- **Status**: identified
- **Priority**: P2
- **Category**: missing-tool
- **Auto-fixable**: no (missing-tool: preamble param)
- **First seen**: Session 47 (2026-08-25, external site-foundry, remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 44 (as the plugin-data workaround), 47, 49
- **Estimated savings**: ~0 calls, ~5-6K output chars per script-heavy build; removes the workaround that caused [BUG-023]
- **Description**: Every `run_script` call is independent, so build-session-specific helpers must be re-sent each time. Session 47 re-declared the same ~700-900 char preamble in **all 7** write scripts — an 8-entry `VariableID:…` map read once from `get_design_system`, an `RGB` fallback table, and an async `V()` variable cache — plus a hand-written `card()` helper in 3 of them. Session 44 hit the same wall under budget pressure and invented a `figma.root.setSharedPluginData("vipwf", …)` module cache re-hydrated per script via `new Function(...)`, costing **8 dedicated calls** and causing [BUG-023] (runtime-generated source bypasses every static check; 15-call parse-error bisect). Session 47's re-send approach is cheaper and far safer, but it is the same gap answered a second way. Two distinct sub-gaps: (a) **generic** helpers — the [TOOL-029] bundle-split (~2KB always-on `prop`/`setCharacters` core, remainder behind `stdlib: false`), deferred in `0af2c9a`; (b) **session-specific** helpers, which no bundle can supply.
- **Proposed fix**: (a) land the [TOOL-029] bundle split so `stdlib: false` does not mean "no `fig.*` at all"; (b) add an optional `preamble` param to `run_script`, cached server-side per fileKey and prepended after the stdlib, so a build defines its token map and helpers once. A `preamble` passes through Figmagent's assembly and deny-list scan, unlike the `new Function` workaround — closing [BUG-023]'s trigger by design rather than by budget relief.
- **Recurred (3rd), and the re-sent preamble now carries a BUG**: Session 49 (2026-08-26, external loupe, remote). **15 of 28** scripts re-declare the same block — a 4-style `Promise.all(loadFontAsync)`, an `sp(instance, props)` component-property setter that resolves bare names against `#`-suffixed keys, an async `I(key, props, w, h)` library-instance importer, `hex`/`solid` colour helpers, and `T()`/`FR()` node factories — roughly 1.5-2KB x 15. Total script volume was **126,546 chars across 28 calls** (avg 4.5K, peak 16.6K). **The new argument for a server-side `preamble` is correctness, not just budget**: after #72 the agent began hand-carrying a *collapse-repair walker* ([AGENT-028]) from script to script, and because each copy was re-typed rather than reused, #101's copy fixed the walker's instance guard while leaving the identical unguarded read in `sp()` — one of the three [BUG-031] crashes. A cached preamble is fixed once; a re-sent one drifts.
- **Note**: schema + assembly change, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [TOOL-029] (parent; verified, with this as the residual), [BUG-023] (caused by the workaround this replaces), [AGENT-025].


- **Recurred (session 62, 2026-09-02, external storybook/"Archer", remote)**: three duplication classes in 28 scripts. (1) An identical 1-line DJB2 hash helper in #3 and #26. (2) The park-and-swap preamble — snapshot each style's `boundVariables` + metrics, unbind `fontFamily`, set `fontName` to `Inter/Regular`, do the work, restore metrics → weight → size → family — written out three times (#17, #18, #23) at ~40 lines each. (3) A `byId`/`byName` text-style map rebuilt from `getLocalTextStylesAsync()` in **11** separate scripts. The park-and-swap block in particular is a stable, [BUG-037]-verified recipe that every custom-font session on this file re-types; it is the strongest case yet for the bundle-split half of [TOOL-029]'s fix, or for promoting it into `edit`'s `textStyleId` path outright.

### [BUG-029] `get_enabled_library_variables` returned 2 collections, then 14, for the same file in one session
- **Status**: identified
- **Priority**: P0
- **Category**: plugin-bug
- **First seen**: Session 48 (2026-08-25, external site-foundry, remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 48
- **Estimated savings**: ~11 calls of direct rework per token-first build, plus the qualitative cost of an entire build's colours being hand-guessed and then replaced
- **Description**: On the remote transport the enabled-library-collection list is **not stable within a session**, and nothing in the response says so. Session 48, file `07plXV7PsHOrLE3hsIS0jS`, no user action in between:

  | Time | Call | Answer |
  |---|---|---|
  | 18:04:35 | #43 `get_enabled_library_variables({})` | `collectionCount: 2` — Foundations, Themes |
  | 18:05:10 | #44 `{collectionKey: Foundations, query: "color"}` | `variableCount: 0`, `variables: []` |
  | 18:09:09 | #69 `{query: "gray"}` | both collections echoed, both `variables: []` |
  | 18:24:53 | #152 `lint` (same API via `describeEnabledLibraryVariables`) | *"tokens come from **2** enabled library collections"* |
  | **18:25:32** | #153 `get_enabled_library_variables({})` | **`collectionCount: 14`** — adds `@wordpress/theme` Color (**257 variables**), Typography, Border, Dimension, Motion + five WPDS (Gutenberg 22.3) collections |

  **Nothing ran between #152 and #153.** Two candidate mechanisms, neither confirmed: (a) Figma enables a library's collections once the file references them, and the agent instantiated library components at 18:07:54 — but that is **17 minutes before** #152 still answered 2, so the timing does not fit a direct causal link; (b) the remote `use_figma` VM's `figma.teamLibrary` state is eventually consistent and under-reports early in a session. The agent's own in-session read was (a) (*"importing the components enabled the real token libraries"*, 18:26:30). This entry deliberately does not pick between them — the reproducible fact is that **`figma.teamLibrary.getAvailableLibraryVariableCollectionsAsync()` returned 2 and then 14 for the same file inside one session**, and both `get_enabled_library_variables` (`styles.js:1203-1209`, `:1246-1255`) and `lint`'s hint (`lint.js:591`, via `describeEnabledLibraryVariables`) present whatever it returns as authoritative.
- **Cost**: acting on the 2-collection answer, the agent concluded the file had no reachable tokens and **hardcoded every colour in the build as a raw RGB literal** across eight frames and ~250 nodes (#72-#151). When the real answer surfaced it spent **#153-#190 — 38 calls, 17 minutes — re-discovering tokens and retro-binding 457 node properties** across ten `edit` calls, plus 8 re-verification screenshots. The binding work was always necessary; the guessing, the replacing, and the second discovery round were not.
- **Proposed fix**: (1) do not present a small collection list as complete — when the count is low **and** the file contains instances of published library components, add a note to both `get_enabled_library_variables` and `lint`'s library hint saying the list can be incomplete early in a remote session and should be re-checked; (2) investigate whether the plugin can force a refresh (a second `getAvailableLibraryVariableCollectionsAsync()` call, or resolving via a known instance's `boundVariables`) before answering; (3) agent guidance — run token discovery **after** the first library component is instantiated, and re-check before concluding a file has no tokens. Priority **P0**: a wrong-but-plausible answer here silently mis-sets every colour decision in a build. Related: [TOOL-024] (same API, same stale answer), [TOOL-026] (empty-collection echo hid that the list itself was short).
- **Note**: correctness/staleness fix — not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist; no auto-plan generated.

### [TOOL-032] `write` cannot bind variables at create time
- **Status**: identified
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: `variables` map on the write node spec, same field names and FIELD_MAP path as edit; binding wins over a literal and the literal is seeded from the resolved value (BUG-043). Recorded on [#133](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/133).
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: missing-tool
- **Auto-fixable**: no (missing-tool: variables on write)
- **First seen**: Session 48 (2026-08-25, external site-foundry, remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 48, 57, 61
- **Estimated savings**: ~5-10 calls per token-first build (removes a mandatory second pass over every created node)
- **Description**: `variables` appears **nowhere** in `src/figmagent_mcp/tools/create.ts`, while `edit` accepts it per node (`apply.ts:132`). Every one of session 48's **457 bindings** therefore had to be a separate `edit` pass over nodes `write` had just created — ten `edit` calls (79, 73, 70, 51, 42, 38, 37, 27, 26 and 14 nodes) whose entire payload is `{nodeId, variables: {...}}`. `write` accepts `fillColor`, `strokeColor`, `cornerRadius`, `fontSize`, padding, spacing and sizing — every visual property *except* the bindings that should own them. On a token-first build the natural call carries structure and bindings together.
- **Proposed fix**: add `variables: z.record(...)` to the node spec in `tools/create.ts`, mirroring `nodeOpSchema`'s field enum in `apply.ts:21-47`, and route it through the same `FIELD_MAP` binding path `apply.js` already uses. The plugin-side binder exists — this is a schema + wiring change on the create path.
- **Note**: Distinct from [BUG-029]: correct discovery ordering removes the *guess-and-replace*, but the second pass stays mandatory until `write` accepts `variables`. Not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist; no auto-plan generated. Related: [TOOL-025], [TOOL-027] (same "field exists on one tool, not the other" family).
- **Recurred (2nd)**: Session 50 (2026-08-26, external site-foundry, remote, same file as S48). **271 bindings across 16 `edit` calls, and 11 of those 16 immediately follow a `write`** — the build rhythm is invariably `write` the frame tree, then `edit` the same node IDs with `variables` (#66→#68, #74→#75, #76→#77, #79→#80/#81, #153→#154, #236/#237→#239 with 28 nodes bound at once). Note the cost is **not** one call per binding — `edit` batches these well (up to 39 node-ops per call) — it is **one extra round trip per `write`**, ~11 calls here. Sharper argument than S48's for shipping it: every newly created node exists briefly in an unbound state, and that is exactly the window in which the post-write assertions and mini-lint run, so the warnings block is computed against values the caller already intended to replace. Fix unchanged (`tools/create.ts` node spec + the existing `FIELD_MAP` binding path).

### [BUG-030] `edit` rejects `variables: {fontWeight}` with a 10,040-char Zod dump and discards the whole batch
- **Status**: identified
- **Priority**: P2
- **Category**: error-message
- **First seen**: Session 48 (2026-08-25, external site-foundry, remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 48, 55
- **Estimated savings**: ~1 call + a 70-op re-serialisation per occurrence; closes a name-collision trap
- **Description**: Session 48 #176 sent a **70-node** `edit` in which several ops carried `variables: {fontSize: "VariableID:…", fontWeight: "VariableID:…"}` and got back `MCP error -32602: Input validation error … {"received": "fontWeight", "code": "invalid_enum_value", "options": [31 fields]}` — **10,040 characters**, and **all 70 operations rejected** over one key. `fontWeight` is genuinely not bindable (Figma binds weight through `fontStyle`, a STRING variable) so the enum at `apply.ts:21-47` is correct — three things about the *failure* are not: (1) **the name collides with a field `edit` does accept** — `apply.ts:93` defines `fontWeight: z.number()` as a direct value, so `fontWeight: 600` is valid while `variables: {fontWeight: …}` is not, and nothing in the schema or description warns about the asymmetry; (2) the response dumps the full 31-option enum plus a repeat per offending node; (3) one bad key discards 69 valid operations — the agent resent the entire batch at #177 with `fontWeight` stripped, and it applied cleanly (70/70).
- **Proposed fix**: (a) ~~name the fix in the rejection — "font weight binds through `fontStyle`…"~~ **superseded, see correction**; (b) collapse the repeated enum dump to one instance; (c) document the direct-value / variable-field name asymmetry in `edit`'s `variables` description at `apply.ts:132`. Same raw-Zod-dump family as [BUG-021] (`grep`); fix both in one pass.
- **CORRECTION (session 53, 2026-09-01)**: this entry's premise — *"`fontWeight` is genuinely not bindable (Figma binds weight through `fontStyle`, a STRING variable) so the enum at `apply.ts:21-47` is correct"* — is **false**. Session 53 bound a FLOAT variable (`font/weight/semibold`, `VariableID:2:450`) to `fontWeight` on 8 TEXT nodes via `setBoundVariable` with `failed: []`; the binding persisted into `boundVariables`, Figmagent's own `read(detail:"full")` resolved it, and it produced `fontWeight: 600`. The (a) remedy was implemented as `VARIABLE_FIELD_ALIASES.fontWeight` (`apply.ts:45-46`) and now ships a wrong fact to every agent. See [TOOL-037], which supersedes (a). Remedies (b) and (c) stand.
- **Note**: error-message fix — not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist; no auto-plan generated. Related: [BUG-021], [BUG-008].

- **Recurred (2nd)**: Session 55 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote). #22 sent `variables: { fill, fontSize, fontFamily, fontWeight }` on three nodes and got `MCP error -32602 … {"received":"fontWeight","code":"invalid_enum_value","options":[…]}` — **the whole batch was discarded, including the three valid bindings in the same call**. #23 re-sent it minus `fontWeight`; #25 was a `run_script` written solely to bind the weight, and #32 a second one. **The schema half has since shipped** (`apply.ts:249-272` now uses `z.record(z.string(), z.string())` + a `superRefine` that names the key and redirects it, commit `040ef9e`) — but the redirect text still carries this entry's false premise, which [TOOL-037] refuted. Verify the no-more-raw-dump half on the next session run against the fixed build; the premise half is [TOOL-037]'s to close.
### [AGENT-028] `resize()` on an auto-layout primary axis silently flips it to `FIXED` — 17 frames collapsed to height 1
- **Status**: identified
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: agent-behavior
- **First seen**: Session 49 (2026-08-26, external loupe, remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 49
- **Estimated savings**: ~14 calls + ~8 minutes per script-built layout session
- **Description**: A build helper set `f.primaryAxisSizingMode='AUTO'` and then `f.resize(o.w, 1)` two lines later. `resize()` on an auto-layout frame's **primary** axis sets that axis's sizing mode to `FIXED`, so every frame created through the helper was pinned at **height 1** with its children clipped to nothing. The symptom presented as a *rendering* fault, not a layout one — `absoluteBoundingBox.height: 1089` but `absoluteRenderBounds.height: 100`, `absoluteRenderBounds: null` on children, and 149-byte (1x1) PNGs from `exportAsync` — so the agent spent **10 calls and ~8 minutes** on the wrong hypothesis: force reflow via `itemSpacing` nudge (#62), inspect children for `visible`/`opacity`/`isMask` (#63), "touch nodes to force rasterization" (#64), `viewport.scrollAndZoomIntoView` then export (#65), and a minimal fresh-node sanity test (#66, which correctly proved the VM healthy). #69 reproduced `h: 1` in isolation; #72 walked the tree re-setting sizing mode where `height<=2` and reported **`fixed: 17`**, `rootH: 3978`. The repair walker was then hand-carried into 5 later scripts (#80, #82, #83, #101, #102) — and caused two of them to crash by recursing into instances ([BUG-031]).
- **Proposed fix**: agent guidance — set auto-layout sizing modes **after** `resize()`, or resize the counter axis only (`resize(w, node.height)`). Add to `figma-guidelines` alongside the existing balloon-frame and width-collapse recipes, and to `run_script`'s description. The structural half is [TOOL-033]: no assertion covers height collapse, and `run_script` emits no assertions at all, so the only feedback channel was a blank screenshot.
- **Note**: agent-knowledge fix — not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist; no auto-plan generated. Sibling to [AGENT-027] (`resize` rescales children with SCALE constraints) — same call, second silent side effect; document both together. Related: [TOOL-033], [BUG-050-family] width-collapse recipes.

### [BUG-031] Remote VM throws `Node not found` for instance-descendant IDs it just returned
- **Status**: identified
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: plugin-bug
- **First seen**: Session 49 (2026-08-26, external loupe, remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 49
- **Estimated savings**: ~4 calls + ~35K chars of re-authored script per script-heavy build
- **Description**: In `run_script` on remote, `.children` and `findAll()` return nested-instance descendants with synthetic `I<a>;<b>;<c>` IDs — and the very next property read on one of those nodes throws. Three build scripts died this way: **#82** `Error: in get_children: Node with id "I14:1842;7:1982;7:1870" not found` (from a tree walker doing `(n.children||[]).forEach(...)`), **#101** `Error: in get_componentProperties: Node with id "I12:3759;2223:498" not found` (from a `setProperties` helper), and **#80** `TypeError: cannot set property 'visible' of undefined` after `menu.findAll(...)` returned entries that didn't resolve. The throw frames (`at get (<input>:35:11)`) place it in the `use_figma` wrapper's node proxy, below user code — the VM hands out an ID it then refuses to resolve. Because scripts are atomic, each failure discarded the whole payload: **#80 (12,498 chars) + #82 (12,248) + #101 (10,558) = 35,304 characters** re-authored as #83 and #102. The agent learned the workaround twice and still shipped it incomplete — #101 added `if(n.type==='INSTANCE')return;` to the walker but left the same unguarded read in `sp()`.
- **Proposed fix**: (a) document the constraint in `run_script`'s tool description — *the remote VM cannot resolve `I…;…` instance-descendant IDs; stop traversal at `type === "INSTANCE"` and do not read properties off nodes below one*; (b) make the `fig.*` stdlib traversal helpers stop at instance boundaries by default, with an explicit opt-in if a caller really wants to descend. Converts a 12K-char atomic loss into a no-op. Distinct from [BUG-011] (instance-override text writes *time out*; these hard-throw) and from [TOOL-030] (reading a published component's property API at all).
- **Note**: stdlib + description change, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [AGENT-028] (whose repair walker triggered two of the three), [TOOL-030], [BUG-011].
- **Scope narrowed — did NOT recur through the first-class `read` tool** (session 51, 2026-08-26, external site-foundry, remote): #32 `read({nodeIds:["I103:1135;66:19","I103:1135;66:17","I66:720;66:692"]})` resolved **three** instance-descendant IDs without error, returning full layout + `componentPropertyReferences` for each. So the defect is specific to the `use_figma` VM's node proxy inside `run_script`, not to `I…;…` IDs in general — and `read`'s resolution path is a **working reference implementation** for the proposed stdlib fix. Narrows the repro and lowers the risk of (b).

### [TOOL-033] `run_script` returns no post-write assertions, and no assertion covers height collapse
- **Status**: identified
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: return contract, loud when missed: write script without `{ nodeIds }` gets a warning; `assertScope: nodeId` for a subtree; add `height_collapse` and mini-lint over the returned nodes. No page-diff auto-tracking. Recorded on [#137](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/137).
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: missing-tool
- **Auto-fixable**: no (mixed: return-contract warning + assertScope param + height_collapse + mini-lint)
- **First seen**: Session 49 (2026-08-26, external loupe, remote transport) — as a first-class cost; noted in passing since session 40
- **Sessions affected**: 40, 41, 44, 46, 47, 49, 50, 54, 55, 58, 59, 61
- **Estimated savings**: ~14 calls per script-built layout session; retires a caveat repeated in five other entries
- **Description**: Two gaps compound. (1) **`run_script` bypasses the assertion layer entirely** — `write`/`edit` append a `warnings:` block (balloon frames, width collapse, FILL-not-applied, font fallback, sibling overlaps) plus mini-lint suggestions, and scripts get none of it. This is already recorded as an aside in [TOOL-025], [TOOL-027], [AGENT-025] and [BUG-028] ("script writes forfeit `edit`'s per-op error reporting, boundary pre-checks and post-write assertions"), but has never been costed on its own. (2) **`assertions.js` has no height-collapse check** — it covers `balloon frame` (100px counter axis) and `width_collapse`, but not an auto-layout frame left at height <= 2, which is what `resize(w, 1)` produces and arguably the more common failure. Session 49 is the clean argument for both: 17 frames collapsed ([AGENT-028]), zero warnings emitted, and the only feedback channel available was a blank screenshot — the slowest possible one, and one that itself failed 22.5% of the time ([BUG-016]).
- **Proposed fix**: (a) add a `height_collapse` assertion mirroring `width_collapse` in `src/figma_plugin/src/assertions.js`; (b) run `runPostWriteAssertions` over nodes touched by a `mode: "write"` `run_script` and append the same `warnings:` block to its result. (b) is the larger change — it needs a way to know which nodes a script touched; a pragmatic first cut is to assert over the subtree of any node the script returns, or accept an optional `assertScope: nodeId` param.
- **Note**: assertion-layer change, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [AGENT-028] (the failure it would have caught), [AGENT-025] (script monoculture makes the blind spot bigger every session), [TOOL-025], [TOOL-027], [BUG-028].
- **Escalated (2nd) — (a) is NOT a `run_script` problem; height collapse fires on the `write` path, where assertions DO run**: Session 50 (2026-08-26, external site-foundry, remote) used **zero `run_script`** and still hit it. `write({fromNodeId:"66:786", parentId:"47:35"})` (#216) and #217 each returned an INSTANCE with **`"height":32`** — against sibling cards at 254–286 created in the same minute (#212–215) — and **neither response carried a `warnings:` block**. Four `write` responses this session reported `height <= 40` (#208, #209, #216, #217); none warned. The agent diagnosed it blind across #219/#220/#221 (one of which blew its own 5,000-char budget and returned nothing usable) before naming it at 18:31:15: *"The picker cards collapsed to 32px — with every card set to stretch, the hug-height row had nothing to size against."* Then #222 `edit`, #223/#224 re-verify, #225 a second `edit`. **~5 calls of pure diagnosis for a defect the tool had already measured and printed.** The failure mode is distinct from [AGENT-028]'s `resize()` flip and needs naming in its own right: **a HUG-height auto-layout row whose children are all `layoutSizingVertical: FILL` has nothing to derive its height from and collapses to padding.** This makes **(a) independently shippable ahead of (b)** — it is a plain addition to `assertions.js` benefiting the path that already runs assertions, with no need to solve script node-tracking first. Priority stays P1; (a) should ship alone.
- **Recurred (3rd) — a third assertion the layer does not have: a variable bound to an INVISIBLE paint**: Session 54 (2026-09-01, external storybook, remote). #38 built a scene with `figma.createComponent()` and bound `--color-background-primary` onto the node's **existing** paint via a helper that reused `cur[0]` as its base — inheriting `visible: false`. #59 read it back: `[{"visible": false, …, "boundVariables": {"color": {"id": "VariableID:2:125"}}}]`. A correctly bound colour that can never render, reported as success. The sibling FRAME built by the same helper in the same script came out `visible: true`, so the shape is easy to miss by inspection. Cost 3 calls (#58 screenshot, #59 diagnose, #60 repair) inside a dark-mode check that should have been 2 calls total ([TOOL-038]). **Add `invisible_paint_binding` to (a)'s list alongside `height_collapse`** — like `height_collapse` it is a plain `assertions.js` addition, and unlike the [AGENT-028] case it fires on the `run_script` path where (b) is still needed to surface it.

- **Recurred (2nd path to the same hole)**: Session 55 #40 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote) returned `nodeIds: [null, null, null]` because of [TOOL-040], so the write-mode postlude ran `fig.check([null,null,null])` inside its bare `try {} catch (_e) {}` and **no assertion ran, with nothing said**. The nodes were created; the warnings block simply vanished. Beyond adding assertions, the postlude should report a `nodeIds` list that is empty or all-null rather than swallowing it — silence there is indistinguishable from a clean run.
- **Recurred (session 58)**: all 13 write operations ran through `run_script`, so the assertion layer never fired. The width-0 label that cost scripts #4-#6 is exactly what `width_collapse` reports on the `write`/`edit` path — and the balloon-frame check would have flagged the focus-ring geometry the session re-fitted by hand twice (#10, #17). This is the 10th session where the blind spot is visible and the 4th where a shipped assertion would have caught the specific failure.


- **Recurred (11th) — and this is the first instance where the missing assertion let a script damage the file beyond the nodes it wrote**: Session 59 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote). #15 returned `is_error: false` having silently dropped a shared text style's `fontWeight` binding and reverted its face ([BUG-044]). The loss surfaced **only** because the agent's own `return` block happened to echo `styleBound` and `styleFont`; a script returning `{ok: true}` would have left `support/label/1` broken for `Accordion` and every other consumer with the session reporting success. Prior recurrences on this entry are about assertions the *write path* would have run on the nodes written; this one argues for a narrower and cheaper check — **a style or variable write that drops an existing binding should never ride an `is_error: false` response**, which is also [BUG-033] remedy (c) applied to styles.

- **Recurred (12th) — the missing assertion is `sibling_overlap`, and it cost a read plus an edit**: Session 61 (2026-09-02, external storybook/"Archer", remote). Four build scripts each placed nodes with explicit page coordinates: trigger at `(520, 480)` is 150px tall, so its bottom edge is 630 — 30px inside the popup set that starts at `y: 600`. Heights are HUG-derived and unknown until after creation, so coordinates computed up front are no protection. `write`/`edit` already ship a sibling-overlap assertion (`assertions.js`); `run_script` runs none, so the collision surfaced only when the agent read the page back (#32) and repaired it (#33). **2 calls, both avoidable by running the existing assertions over a script's returned `nodeIds`.**

### [TOOL-034] `write` cannot clone more than one node per call — `fromNodeId` is mutually exclusive with `nodes`
- **Status**: identified
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: missing-batch-tool
- **First seen**: Session 50 (2026-08-26, external site-foundry, remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 50
- **Estimated savings**: ~20 calls per component-set / state-matrix build
- **Description**: Cloning N nodes costs N `write` calls, always. There is no `count`/`repeat` param, and the schema **rejects** the array form outright: `create.ts:183` describes `fromNodeId` as *"Mutually exclusive with 'nodes'"* and `:204` returns `"Error: 'fromNodeId' cannot be combined with 'nodes'."` The handler branches on `fromNodeId` before the array path (`create.ts:197–255`) and sends a single `clone_node` command. Session 50 made **30 clone calls out of 76 `write`s, 27 of them inside 7 consecutive same-purpose runs** (5, 3, 3, 5, 2, 6, 3), at a ~5-second remote round-trip each. Both batch shapes are needed: **same source × N** (`#83–87`: `52:14` cloned five times into `39:443`; `#155–157`, `#185–187`, `#230–232`: `71:14` ×3 each) and **N distinct source→parent pairs** (`#195–199`: five sources into five parents; `#212–217`: six library-card instances into `43:26`/`47:35`).
- **Proposed fix**: accept `nodes: [{ fromNodeId, parentId?, name?, x?, y?, fillColor?, cornerRadius? }, …]` — the clone spec already permits exactly those fields — and/or add `count: z.number()` to the single-clone path. The fan-out loop already exists for the create path (`Promise.all(nodes.map(createOne))`, `create.ts:255`); the clone path needs the same treatment plus removal of the mutual-exclusion guard.
- **Verification**: `bun run lint` · `bun run test` · `bun run build:plugin` · `write({nodes:[{fromNodeId:"1:2",parentId:"1:9"},{fromNodeId:"1:3",parentId:"1:9"}]})` returns two clone summaries.
- **Note**: matches the `missing-batch-tool` pattern but the longest run is 6, under the 20-consecutive-call auto-plan trigger — no auto-plan generated. Related: [TOOL-021], [TOOL-026] (same batched-half / un-batched-half asymmetry).

### [TOOL-035] `edit` cannot set per-side stroke weight — forces delete-and-re-clone of finished variants
- **Status**: identified
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: missing-tool
- **First seen**: Session 50 (2026-08-26, external site-foundry, remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 50
- **Estimated savings**: ~5 calls, plus the re-authoring of every override on the destroyed nodes
- **Description**: Figma exposes `strokeTopWeight` / `strokeBottomWeight` / `strokeLeftWeight` / `strokeRightWeight` on rect-like nodes. **None of those four strings appears anywhere in `src/`** — `edit` offers only `strokeWeight: z.number().positive()` (`tools/apply.ts:81`), which writes all four sides at once. Session 50's user hand-edited the `State=Default` variant of a `Form/Site details` COMPONENT_SET to give its `Card.Header` a bottom-only stroke; the agent could not replicate it and said so twice, then **deleted three finished variants and re-cloned them from the edited master purely to inherit the property**: *"I deleted and re-cloned Attention / Validating / Error from your edited `State=Default` rather than patching them. Your Card.Header has a bottom-only stroke, and the edit API can only set stroke weight on all four sides at once; cloning was the only way."* (18:32:58). Cost: `#184` delete ×4 → `#185–187` three clones → `#188` `read` → `#189` `write` → `#190–191` two `edit`s re-applying overrides = **8 calls** where ~2 `edit`s would do — and three variants' worth of correct overrides thrown away and re-authored. A bottom-only header rule is standard card chrome, not an exotic need.
- **Proposed fix**: add `strokeTopWeight`, `strokeBottomWeight`, `strokeLeftWeight`, `strokeRightWeight` as direct-value fields on `nodeOpSchema` in `tools/apply.ts`, plus the corresponding setters in `apply.js`. Third member of the same `nodeOpSchema` field-gap family — **ship in one pass with [TOOL-025]** (`letterSpacing`/`textCase`/`minWidth`/`maxWidth`/`lineHeight`/`textDecoration`/`visible`) **and [TOOL-027]** (`layoutPositioning`/`clipsContent`).
- **Verification**: `bun run lint` · `bun run test` · `bun run build:plugin` · `edit({nodes:[{nodeId, strokeBottomWeight:1, strokeTopWeight:0}]})` leaves the other sides untouched.
- **Note**: missing-tool (schema + setter surface) — not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist; no auto-plan generated. Related: [TOOL-025], [TOOL-027].

### [AGENT-029] A stated fix that fails once is a wrong diagnosis — change strategy, not parameters
- **Status**: identified
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: agent-behavior
- **First seen**: Session 51 (2026-08-26, external site-foundry, remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 51, 55 (executed correctly)
- **Estimated savings**: ~4 calls per session where a fix-stating error is wrong; generalises to every `fail(message, fix)` site
- **Description**: The project rule "no user-facing error without a stated fix" has a failure mode nothing currently guards: an error whose stated fix is **wrong** converts a fix-stating message into a retry trap. Session 51's `screenshot` guard asserts *"the rendered payload exceeded the ~4MB return cap. Fix: re-request at a lower `scale` … or use `format: "SVG"`"*. The agent complied precisely and burned four calls: `43:14` at `scale: 1` → error, at `scale: 0.5` → **byte-identical** error; `103:1135` at `scale: 1` → error, at `format: "SVG"` → **byte-identical** error. The existing guard, [AGENT-001] ("after 2 consecutive **identical** errors on the same tool, stop retrying"), does **not** fire here — the agent changed its input each time, exactly as instructed, so the calls are not identical. The gap is precisely the case where an agent is being obedient rather than repetitive.
- **Proposed fix**: extend the CLAUDE.md fail-fast rule to cover prescribed-fix exhaustion — *if an error returns unchanged after you applied the fix it stated, treat the diagnosis as wrong: stop tuning that parameter and change strategy (different tool, different decomposition, or report the blocker).* Strictly stronger than [AGENT-001] and cheap to state. Pair with a code-side discipline note: a `fail(message, fix)` site must not assert a cause it did not measure — prefer "the export returned no image data (cause not determined)" over a confident wrong cause, since agents act on the text.
- **Note**: agent-behavior/CLAUDE.md change, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [AGENT-001] (the narrower identical-call rule it extends), [BUG-016] (the error text that motivated it).

- **Executed correctly, three times, in session 55** (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote): (a) on [BUG-032]'s wrong "style not found" message the agent abandoned `textStyleId` after **one** failure rather than permuting the ID format as session 53 did for five calls; (b) on [BUG-039]'s wrong property-type fix it read past the stated remedy, recognised the font cause in Figma's own message, and pivoted on the next call; (c) on [BUG-016] it tried `scale` once and `SVG` once, then **stopped changing arguments and started changing the scene** — which is the only reason that entry now has a discriminator. This is the behaviour the entry asks for, and it is worth recording that it produced a genuine root-cause finding, not just a faster recovery.
### [AGENT-030] `^`-anchored `grep` name patterns miss slash-prefixed component names
- **Status**: identified
- **Priority**: P2
- **Category**: agent-behavior
- **First seen**: Session 51 (2026-08-26, external site-foundry, remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 51
- **Estimated savings**: ~1 call per discovery pass that anchors a component-name regex
- **Description**: Session 51 #14 searched `grep({name: "^Blueprint (card|placeholder image)", type:["COMPONENT","COMPONENT_SET"], scope:"DOCUMENT"})` and matched **1 of 2** targets. The miss was `Picker/Blueprint card` — Figma component names routinely carry a `Group/` prefix, which a `^` anchor silently excludes. #16 retried with the bare substring `Blueprint` and found both. The agent self-corrected in one step, so the cost is small, but the failure is silent: a partial match looks exactly like a complete one, and `matchCount: 1` reads as success.
- **Proposed fix**: agent-side guidance in the `grep` tool description — *component names are frequently namespaced with a `Group/` prefix; prefer an unanchored substring for discovery and add `^` only when you already know the full name.* Optionally tool-side: when an anchored `name` pattern yields matches, note in `meta` that anchoring was applied, so a thin result set is legible as possibly-truncated-by-pattern.
- **Note**: description/guidance change, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated.

### [TOOL-036] `lint` reports a passing summary on a scan it skipped, and cannot verify library bindings
- **Status**: identified
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: with pagination (TOOL-043) the summary carries `shown`/`total` over the full scan so a closing triage can name what it did not inspect; library-binding half unchanged. Recorded on [#142](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/142).
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: missing-tool
- **Auto-fixable**: no (mixed: summary shape + library bindings)
- **First seen**: Session 52 (2026-08-27, external site-foundry, remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 48, 52, 56, 61 (the verification half of [TOOL-024]; [AGENT-020] is the agent-behavior ancestor)
- **Estimated savings**: ~4 calls per library-token session, and one class of false "design is clean" conclusion
- **Description**: On a file whose tokens all come from enabled libraries, `lint` short-circuits and returns `summary: {totalNodesScanned: 0, totalIssues: 0, byProperty: {}, bySeverity: {}, autoFixed: 0}`, `issues: []`, `truncated: false` with **`is_error` unset** — plus the [TOOL-024] routing `message` explaining that there are no local variables. The prose is correct; the **structured verdict contradicts it**. `totalIssues: 0` alongside `truncated: false` is exactly the shape of a clean bill of health, and CLAUDE.md explicitly trains agents to *"prefer branching on `is_error` over parsing content text."* An agent that follows that guidance reads a skipped scan as a passing one. Session 52 (#222) passed both board roots and got a `roots` breakdown of two zeroes after building ~2 boards' worth of nodes against library tokens; session 48 (#152, #188) got eight zeroes after binding **457 node properties**. In neither session could `lint` confirm a single binding the agent had just made.
- **Proposed fix**: two parts, independently shippable. (1) **Make the verdict match the prose** — when `lintDesign` short-circuits on a library-only file, do not emit a passing summary: set `totalNodesScanned: null` (or add `skipped: true` / `skippedReason: "library-only"`) at both the top level and per-root, so a summary-only reader cannot mistake it for a pass. Cheap, no new capability. (2) **Make `lint` match library variables** — the capability [TOOL-024]'s implementation note explicitly deferred ("this makes both tools explain and route — it does NOT make `lint` match library variables"). Two sessions have now ended with a large binding pass that nothing could verify; on library-backed files, which is the normal case for real design-system work, `lint` is currently inert.
- **Note**: (1) is a result-serialization change and (2) is a new detection branch — neither is in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist (sync-to-async / type-coercion / description-only / lint-scope-filter / boundary-guard / assertion); no auto-plan generated. Related: [TOOL-024] (routing half, verified), [AGENT-020], [BUG-026] (`is_error: false` on a real failure).

- **Session 56's unmeasured triage is now measured, and it was right (session 61)**: this entry flags session 56 for closing a build on the claim that its 93 lint issues were "set-wrapper chrome and deliberate literal opacities" — asserted from summary buckets with 33 issues never returned. Session 61 ran the same shape of claim against a **full, uncapped** issue list (21 issues under `maxIssues: 60`) and the breakdown is exactly that: 14 set-wrapper chrome, 7 opacity literals, 0 actionable. The triage heuristic is sound. **The concern this entry raises survives anyway** — a category that reliably accounts for two-thirds of findings is exactly the cover a real finding passes unnoticed under, which is why [TOOL-049] proposes removing the category from the output rather than trusting the triage.

### [AGENT-031] Stop the `scale` ladder at two — change a variable, don't shrink a number
- **Status**: identified
- **Priority**: P2
- **Category**: agent-behavior
- **First seen**: Session 52 (2026-08-27, external site-foundry, remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 48, 51, 52, 54, 55 (executed correctly), 56, 57, 58, 61
- **Estimated savings**: ~8 calls per verification-heavy session
- **Description**: [BUG-016]'s error text names `scale` as the first remedy, so agents ladder it downward on failure. Session 52 ran `141:26` through **six** attempts — 1.5, 0.9, 0.5, 0.35, 0.55, 0.4 — a 19× reduction in pixel area, with no success and no new information after the second. Sessions 48 (0.35 fails while 0.28 succeeds) and 51 (`scale: 1` → `0.5`, byte-identical error) show the same shape. Because the failure tracks **render bounds and response survival**, not the `scale` parameter, each additional rung is a coin flip dressed as a bisection.
- **Proposed fix**: two lines of guidance, ideally carried in the `screenshot` tool description so it reaches every agent. (1) *"On a `screenshot` failure, retry at most once, then export a child node. Do not run a descending `scale` sequence — `scale` is not the governing variable."* (2) The positive counterpart, which session 52 demonstrated twice and which generalises well past this bug: **when a stated fix does not work, hold the call constant and change one property of the target instead.** The agent cleared `effects` on a COMPONENT and re-ran a byte-identical `screenshot` that then succeeded (isolating [BUG-016]'s real trigger), and ran `setCurrentPageAsync` before a byte-identical `import_library_components` payload that went 0/17 → 17/17 (verifying [BUG-018]'s fix). Both took one call and produced a shippable result, where parameter-laddering produced none in three sessions.
- **Note**: agent-behavior / tool-description change, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [BUG-016] (the error text that causes the laddering), [AGENT-001] (the narrower identical-call rule), [AGENT-029].
- **Recurred (4th) — and the cause is now pinned to a MISSING message, not a misleading one**: Session 54 (2026-09-01, external storybook, remote) laddered 0.75 (batch) -> 0.6 -> **1.0**, the third rung going *upward* — the clearest demonstration yet that the agent had no model of the governing variable and was permuting a number. What triggered it is diagnostic: the batch call returned `Exported 0 node(s): none` with **no fix text at all** ([BUG-016] v5 item 2b), so there was nothing to follow. **Shipping 2b is therefore a partial fix for this entry too** — the ladder starts in the gap where guidance should be. Recovery was otherwise correct: after the third failure the agent switched to a structural `read` and never laddered again, and `screenshot` succeeded 5 more times in the session.

- **Executed correctly, and it paid off**: Session 55 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote) stopped the ladder at two — `scale: 2` (#61) then `format: "SVG"` (#62) — and then changed the scene instead of the number. Removing the drop shadow from the subtree (#68) made the identical failing call succeed (#69), which is [BUG-016]'s first isolated discriminator in 17 sessions. Concrete addition for this entry: **"remove effects from the subtree" belongs on the diagnostic ladder ahead of any further `scale` attempt.**
- **Executed correctly (3rd consecutive session), with the best outcome on record**: Session 56 (2026-09-01, same file). All three `screenshot` failures were handled by exporting a **child node** first — #62 (Popup set) → #63/#65 (individual variants, both OK at scale 1.0); #69 (Combobox set) → #70/#71 (both variants) — and exactly **one** scale step was ever taken (#71 at 1.0 → #72 at 0.5, then stop). No ladder, no permutation. Failure rate 16.7% (3 of 18), the lowest across 18 sessions of [BUG-016].
- **Executed correctly (5th consecutive session)**: Session 58 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote). On [BUG-016]'s failure at `42:494`, the agent took **exactly one** scale step (0.55 → 0.4) and then stopped changing the parameter, switching to five child-node exports at `scale: 2` — all successful within 13 seconds. The second failure (`23:3` @ scale 1) was cleared in a single step by exporting child `23:6`. No ladder, no verbatim retry, no `SVG` detour.

- **Executed correctly, 6th consecutive session (61)** — and the fallback produced a *better* artifact than the failed call would have. `62:73` cleared in one scale step; `63:75` got exactly two attempts (0.6 → 0.5) before the agent abandoned the argument and exported child variants at scale 1.0 instead. Two 180px-wide variants at full scale are more legible than a 6-variant set at half. The rule's value is no longer just "stop wasting calls" — on this shape, changing the subject strictly dominates shrinking the number.

### [BUG-032] `edit` reports "Text style not found or not cached" when the real failure is an unloadable font
- **Status**: identified
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: plugin-bug
- **First seen**: Session 53 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 53, 55
- **Estimated savings**: ~6 calls per session on any custom-font file; keeps the agent on `edit` instead of defecting to `run_script`
- **Description**: `edit({ textStyleId })` failed 4/4 with `Text style not found or not cached: S:1ae6a2e7ba3e6949ffed2681446e6c6c2b397a0a,` on a style ID `get_design_system` had returned **9 seconds earlier**. The style existed; the ID was correct. Root cause is two lines in `src/figma_plugin/src/commands/apply.js`: the style pre-load loop (`:856-869`) wraps `await figma.loadFontAsync(style.fontName)` in a bare `catch (_e)` commented *"Style load failure will be caught per-node later"* — when the font is unavailable (see [BUG-033]) the throw is swallowed **before** `styleCache[id] = style`, and the per-node check at `:200-201` then reports a resolved style as missing. What is "caught per-node later" is a different and false claim. Same shape in `applyEffectStyle` (`:226`).
- **Cost shape — the message sends the agent after the wrong variable**: taking the error literally, the agent spent four calls permuting the ID format (trailing comma #61, no comma #62, re-fetch the exact string #63, batch re-send #64, bare key without the `S:` prefix #65), got the byte-identical error every time, then abandoned `edit` for `run_script` (#66/#67). A textbook [AGENT-029] trigger caused entirely by a wrong error message.
- **Proposed fix**: record the reason instead of discarding it — `styleErrors[styleKeys[i]] = e.message` in the catch, then `applyTextStyle`/`applyEffectStyle` throw `fail(styleErrors[styleId] || "Text style not found: " + styleId, <fix>)`. The project's "no user-facing error without a stated fix" rule extends to not stating a **wrong** cause.
- **Note**: error-handling fix, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist (sync-to-async / type-coercion / description-only / lint-scope-filter / boundary-guard / assertion) — no auto-plan generated. Related: [BUG-033] (the underlying font failure), [BUG-010] (the `update_styles` font pre-load family), [BUG-008] (errors that misreport their own cause).

- **Recurred (2nd) — same style ID, same file, independent session**: Session 55 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote, overlapping session 54). #21 sent `textStyleId: "S:1ae6a2e7ba3e6949ffed2681446e6c6c2b397a0a,"` copied verbatim from `get_design_system` **97 seconds earlier** (#14 returned it as `{"id":"S:1ae6…a0a,","name":"body/1","fontFamily":"PP Neue Montreal",…}`) and all three TEXT children failed with the byte-identical `Text style not found or not cached`. Confirms the trigger is the file's font, not a one-off.
- **The fix shipped mid-session and is not yet verified in the wild**: `failStyleLookup` (`src/figma_plugin/src/commands/apply.js:329-350`) now distinguishes a rejected id from an unloadable font and states opposite remedies — commit `d50708b`, 2026-09-01 23:18 UTC, **fifteen minutes after this call** and after this session's server process had started. Session 55 is therefore a pre-fix reproduction, not a regression. **Next session on a custom-font file is the verification target**: the expected message names the font, not the style.
- **Recovery improved sharply without any tooling change**: session 53 burned five calls permuting the ID format; session 55 abandoned `textStyleId` on the very next call and set the type properties directly (#22 → #23 → #25). [AGENT-029] executed correctly on the identical error.
### [BUG-033] The remote VM cannot load a file's own custom fonts, and every failure mode misreports it
- **Status**: identified
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: auto park-and-restore on remote, always on, node-level by default; response carries `font_parked`; parking failure fails with the true cause. Full contract in #145. Recorded on [#145](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/145).
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: plugin-bug
- **Auto-fixable**: no (design work: park-and-restore mode)
- **First seen**: Session 53 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 53 (both halves), 54, 55, 56, 57, 58, 59, 60
- **Estimated savings**: ~7 calls per session; unblocks the entire typography path on remote for custom-font files
- **Description**: `figma.listAvailableFontsAsync()` (#21) returned **zero** matches for `PP Neue Montreal` — the family every text style, `font/family/*` variable and TEXT node in the file references — out of 1,938 fonts available in the VM (#22). The headless `use_figma` VM has no access to fonts installed on the user's machine or licensed to the Figma desktop client, so **any file using a non-Google/non-system typeface is affected**, which is most design-system files. Five separate operations broke on it, each discovered independently: `set_textAutoResize` on a new TEXT node (#20, #23), `setBoundVariable("fontFamily")` (#25, #55), and `setTextStyleIdAsync` (#66). The plugin transport is unaffected — the desktop client has the fonts.
- **Two of the five failures shipped as successes**: #25 returned `{"boundTexts": 12, "warnings": [2 x "unloaded font"]}` with `is_error: false` (two of twelve bindings did not happen; the count says twelve), and #66 returned four `"…FAILED: …unloaded font…"` entries plus its own `verify` block reading `styleId=none` on all four nodes, also `is_error: false`. The #25 miss survived 15 calls until a repair pass (#40) found it.
- **Verified workaround (worth documenting as-is)**: the *binding* survives even though the font cannot render. Temporarily set the target (node or style) to an available font, perform the write, then re-bind the `fontFamily` variable **last** — session 53 #67 did exactly this and verified `11:32 style=body/1 font=PP Neue Montreal Regular size=14` on all four nodes. The agent converged on it in three attempts (#24 "bind fontFamily last", #40 repair pass, #67 full swap-write-rebind).
- **Proposed fix**: (a) in the shared font loader (`src/figma_plugin/src/helpers.js`), when `loadFontAsync` throws, check `listAvailableFontsAsync` and `fail()` with the true cause and the swap-write-rebind remedy rather than re-throwing Figma's "call loadFontAsync first" text — which is unactionable when the font does not exist; (b) document the pattern in the `run_script` and `edit` descriptions; (c) a dropped `fontFamily` bind must not ride an `is_error: false` response inside a `warnings` array. Fixing (a) also fixes [BUG-032]'s trigger.
- **Second-half evidence (calls #68-#86)**: #74 tried five face spellings (`Semibold`, `Semi Bold`, `SemiBold`, `Medium`, `Regular`) and **all five failed** — the family is absent, not the face. A **sixth** operation broke on it that the first half had not reached: `insertChild` (#75) — a node carrying a missing font cannot be re-parented or cloned at all, which is why the six stuck labels could not simply be rebuilt. The downstream consequences are now tracked separately as [BUG-034] (weight bindings go inert) and [BUG-035] (the face-name spelling that makes them invisible).
- **Note**: font-loading family — same lineage as [BUG-004] (import), [BUG-007] (create), [BUG-010] (update_styles), but a distinct root cause: those are load-order bugs, this is font **absence**. Not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist. Related: [BUG-034], [BUG-035], [AGENT-032].
- **Recurred (2nd) — confirmed on a second component in the same file, and the workaround improved**: Session 54 (2026-09-01, same file, two minutes after 53). #40 asked the VM directly for all three faces and got `The font family "PP Neue Montreal" does not exist` x3 with `avail: []`; #42 confirmed **zero** matches out of **8,927** fonts.
