Four operations broke, **two of them silently** (`is_error: false`): #39 `setTextStyleIdAsync` returned `"FAIL … unloaded font"` for **all 8** TEXT nodes and reported success; #67 returned `how: "FAIL async=… | sync=…"`, `style: ""` for both nodes and reported success. Hard failures: #41 (`edit({textStyleId})` x2) and #43/#44 (`set_lineHeight`, `setBoundVariable`). Remedy (c) — *a dropped write must not ride an `is_error: false` response* — is now the most-recurring half of this entry.
- **The style-level swap dominates the node-level one**: #68 swapped the **style's own** `fontName` to an available family, applied the style, and re-bound the style's `fontFamily` — `applied: ["23:5 style=set", "24:4 style=set"]` with `after.face: "PP Neue Montreal/Regular"` and `boundVariables` intact. One operation fixes every node on the style and **keeps the style link**, where the node-level swap-write-rebind recorded above fixes one node at a time and (per [AGENT-032]) leaves the type on per-node properties. Tracked as [BUG-037], which is the message change this implies.

- **Recurred (3rd), with two measurements worth keeping**: Session 55 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote). #37 (`write`) and #38 (`run_script`) both failed with `in appendChild: unloaded font "PP Neue Montreal Regular"`; #44 failed 8 more ops through `setProperties` (see [BUG-039]). The in-VM probe (#39) returned `{"ppStyles": [], "totalFonts": 8927, "loadResult": "…The font family \"PP Neue Montreal\" does not exist.\n\nFonts from text styles:\n- PP Neue Montreal (Regular, Medium, Semibold, Extrabold)…"}`. **(a) The VM's font inventory is not stable**: 8,927 fonts here against session 53's 1,938 on the same file, so a workaround validated by inventory in one session cannot be assumed in the next. **(b) Figma's own error enumerates the family under "Fonts from text styles"** — the VM knows exactly which family the file needs and that it cannot load it, which is the information Figmagent's own errors should be surfacing.
- **The swap-write-rebind order confirmed working, with a refinement that belongs in [BUG-035]**: #85 re-authored two group labels on `SF Pro / Semibold` — chosen because its **style name spells "Semibold" the way the target face does** — then re-bound the `fontFamily` variable last. Readback: `{"family":"PP Neue Montreal","style":"Semibold"}` on both. **The family binding restores the real face name and carries the style axis with it**, so the donor face must match on style *spelling*, not on numeric weight. #86 audited all 35 TEXT nodes on the page and every one resolved to `PP Neue Montreal` Regular or Semibold.
- **Standing cost**: 3 of session 55's 19 write scripts (#40, #45, #85) existed only to perform this dance.
- **Recurred (4th) — and the swap-write-rebind recipe is now known to be incomplete**: Session 56 (2026-09-01, same file, 22 minutes after 55). A 4th component, three more hard failures on three more operations — #32 `set_textAutoResize`, #66 `appendChild`, #67 `set_fontName` — plus #57/#60 through `setProperties` ([BUG-039]). #52 asked the VM for all four faces (`Regular`, `Semibold`, `Medium`, `Book`) and got `The font family "PP Neue Montreal" does not exist.` for every one, each followed by Figma's own `Fonts from text styles: - PP Neue Montreal (Regular, Medium, Semibold, Extrabold)` — corroborating observation (b) from session 55 on a second occasion. **The new fact is [BUG-040]**: the swap-write-rebind order leaves the node measuring at **width 0** for four of five donor families tested, including `SF Pro`, the donor this entry adopted in session 55. Remedy (b) must now carry an explicit `resize()` after the rebind. Standing cost held at 3 of 24 write scripts (#58, #59, #68) existing only to perform the dance — the same 3-script tax measured in session 55.

- **Recurred (5th) — and a NEW code site the previous four never reached**: Session 57 (2026-09-01, same file, running concurrently with session 56). #20 called `fig.loadFont('PP Neue Montreal', 'Regular')` and `…'Semi Bold'` — the *stdlib helper the project asks script authors to use* — and **both resolved successfully**, because `loadFontWithFallback` catches the throw and substitutes `Inter`. The script died 30 lines later on `set_fontName`, discarding a ~120-line atomic scene build. Proposed fix (a) on this entry — *"when `loadFontAsync` throws, check `listAvailableFontsAsync` and `fail()` with the true cause"* — **cannot fire here**: the throw is swallowed before any caller sees it. Filed separately as [BUG-041] because the defect is the wrapper's silent success, not the font's absence. **Recovery was the best of the five sessions**: no face-spelling ladder, one memory read (#21), one probe (#22), one corrected script (#23) that worked first try — [AGENT-029] executed correctly against an error whose stated fix was the call that had just failed.
- **[BUG-037]'s style-level swap is now 3-for-3, and its cost profile is clear**: #23 parked the shared `body/1` style on `Inter`, built and styled every TEXT node, then restored `fontWeight`, `lineHeight`, `letterSpacing` and — last — the `fontFamily` binding; readback confirmed `{family: "PP Neue Montreal", style: "Regular"}` with `bound: [fontSize, fontFamily, fontWeight]` intact. **The donor choice mattered and [BUG-040] did not fire**: the donor was `Inter`, the one family session 56's five-donor experiment found never measures 0 — where `SF Pro`, which this entry and [BUG-035] currently recommend, is one of the four that do. Independent corroboration of [BUG-040] from a separate run, and grounds to change the recommended donor to `Inter`. **New hazard worth stating**: the style-level swap mutates a file-wide style for the duration of the script, and session 57 performed it while session 56 was writing the same file — prefer the node-level swap when another session may be active.
- **Recurred (6th), and the parking workaround is now mature enough to cost**: Session 58 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote). `PP Neue Montreal` unavailable again; VM reported **1,938** families, matching session 53 exactly against session 55's 8,927 — the inventory is not stable between sessions, so a donor cannot be cached ([TOOL-045]). Script #20 shows the workaround's full operational shape: to insert Button instances into the Alert Dialog popup, it had to **park 68 non-instance TEXT nodes across two pages** onto style-matched VM faces (`Semibold` → Noto Sans New Tai Lue, `Regular` → Inter, `Extrabold` → Noto Looped Thai), unbinding `fontFamily` on each, perform the structural surgery, then re-bind — because `createInstance`, `insertChild` **and** `setProperties` all throw `unloaded font` otherwise. That is three more API surfaces added to this entry's list of five. The consequence is architectural, not just costly: **any structural edit on a custom-font file is `run_script`-only**, because no first-class tool can park and restore fonts around a write. Worth considering an `allowFontFallback: true` / park-and-restore mode on `write`/`edit` as the first-class counterpart.


- **Recurred (7th) — and for the first time the failure lands on a FIRST-CLASS TOOL, not a script**: Session 59 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote). `#22 edit({nodes:[{nodeId:"35:4", index:0}]})` — reorder one variant so the MD size sits first in the set — hard-failed with ``in insertChild: unloaded font "PP Neue Montreal Semibold". Please call figma.loadFontAsync(…) and await the returned promise first.`` Every prior `insertChild`/`appendChild`/`createInstance` failure on this entry (S53 #75, S55 #37/#38, S56 #66, S58 #20) happened **inside `run_script`**, where the agent could park fonts by hand. `edit` has no park-and-restore mechanism, so the stated fix is unreachable from the surface that raised it, and a one-field reorder cost a 30-line script (#23: unbind `fontFamily` → park the shared style on `Inter` → two `insertChild` calls → restore face, weight binding, line height, family binding). **This is the direct evidence for the `allowFontFallback: true` / park-and-restore proposal recorded in the session-58 note**: route `edit`'s structural operations (`index`, `parentId`) through it and the architectural claim — *any structural edit on a custom-font file is `run_script`-only* — stops being true. Cheaper interim: `edit` should not relay Figma's "call `loadFontAsync` first" text for a family that `listAvailableFontsAsync` does not contain, which is remedy (a) applied at the `edit` boundary rather than the script one. **Agent recovery was the best of the seven**: one failure, no retry, no face-spelling ladder, straight to a script that worked first try.

- **Recurred (8th) — the first time it costs a DELIVERABLE, not just calls**: Session 60 (2026-09-02, same file, ~4 hours after 59). Three hard failures on the same family: `run_script` #4 (`createInstance` + `appendChild`), `run_script` #7 (`clone()` + `appendChild`), and — the **second first-class-tool occurrence** after session 59's `edit({index})` — `write({fromNodeId: "11:60", parentId: "63:322"})`, which returned `in appendChild: unloaded font "PP Neue Montreal Regular" … "PP Neue Montreal Semi Bold"`. Add `write`'s `fromNodeId` clone to the list of structural operations that need the `allowFontFallback` / park-and-restore mode; cloning a text-bearing subtree into a new parent is precisely what that mode exists for.
- **The escalation is the new fact.** Every prior recurrence paid ~3-7 calls and then completed via the park/swap dance. Here the agent judged parking fonts across three cloned rows x four frames out of scope for a prototype-flow build, put the choice to the user via `AskUserQuestion` (recommending a switch to the plugin transport, which is correct), and the user cut the scope — the single-select prototype flow was **never built**. Eight sessions of "costs ~7 calls" has become "costs the feature".
- **One dead hypothesis a correct error message would have prevented**: probe #6 established that append into an **auto-layout** frame fails, which the agent read as "auto layout is the trigger". #7 rebuilt the whole 2,314-character script with `layoutMode: 'NONE'` and manual positioning and failed at the same `container.appendChild(row)` call. **`appendChild` of a node carrying an unavailable font fails regardless of the parent's layout mode** — a fact this entry did not previously record, and one that costs a full script rewrite to discover empirically.
- **Recovery was again best-in-class** (alongside session 57): no retry of a failed call, three distinct hypotheses each tested once, a first-class cross-check confirming the blocker was not script-specific, then an honest scope escalation. [AGENT-029] executed correctly for the third consecutive session.

- **Absent for the first time on this file (session 61)** — a 21-variant, ~94-node build with 17 text nodes on a custom-typeface file, and **zero font failures**. Not a fix: the agent pre-loaded every remedy this entry and its siblings produced ([BUG-040]'s donor face, [BUG-044]'s double-assign, an explicit `loadFontAsync` for both faces up front) out of the project's `figma-remote-vm-gotchas.md`. Worth recording as the control case — the defect is unchanged, but its cost is fully absorbable by documented workarounds, which sets the bar the eventual fix has to beat.


- **Recurred (6th) — and at a third code site (session 62, 2026-09-02, same file, remote)**: `loadFontOrFail` (`src/figma_plugin/src/commands/styles.js:8-17`), the guard on `create_styles`/`update_styles`, catches any `loadFontAsync` throw and states the fix *"pass a fontFamily/fontStyle pair that exists exactly as Figma lists it (e.g. fontFamily: 'Inter', fontStyle: 'Semi Bold' — not 'SemiBold' or a weight number)"*. That is the right message for a **misspelled face** and the wrong one for an **absent family** — the case this entry is about, and the case that holds for every text style on this file. It is also the reason [TOOL-050] exists: an agent reading `create_styles`' description concludes the tool cannot express a variable-bound style on an unavailable family, when in fact authoring on `Inter/Regular` and binding `fontFamily` works. This entry's proposed fix (consult `listAvailableFontsAsync` and `fail()` with the true cause) should be applied at `loadFontOrFail` as well as the sites already named.

### [TOOL-037] `edit` cannot bind `fontWeight`, and the rejection message states a false reason

- **Status**: implemented — `fontWeight` added to `FIELD_MAP` (`styles.js`, fixes `edit` and `fig.bindVariable` in one change) and to `apply.ts`'s `variableFieldEnum`; the false `fontStyle` redirect and the "not bindable" description text are gone. Test in `tests/criteria-and-binding-errors.test.ts`.
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: missing-tool
- **First seen**: Session 53 second half (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 48 (as the mis-diagnosed [BUG-030]), 53, 55, 56, 61
- **Estimated savings**: ~6 calls/session on any weight-tokened file, and retires a tool description that teaches a false fact
- **Description**: `apply.ts:45-46` (`VARIABLE_FIELD_ALIASES.fontWeight`) and `apply.ts:207` (the `variables` description) both assert *"`fontWeight` is a number, settable directly but not bindable — font weight binds through `fontStyle`, a STRING variable holding e.g. 'Bold'."* Session 53 disproves it. The Archer file's design system binds a **FLOAT** variable `font/weight/semibold` (`VariableID:2:450`) to `fontWeight` on TEXT nodes, and Figma accepts it: #70 called `setBoundVariable('fontWeight', semibold)` on 8 Label nodes and returned `failed: []`; #73 read `fontWeight` back inside `boundVariables` alongside `fills`/`fontSize`/`fontFamily`; **#72 — Figmagent's own `read(detail: "full")` — resolved it into `defs.vars` as `v4: VariableID:2:450`**; #84 measured `w=600` on the nodes where the face lookup fired. The serializer reports a binding the writer refuses to make.
- **Root cause — blocked twice, explained once, wrongly**: `FIELD_MAP` (`src/figma_plugin/src/commands/styles.js:1166-1168`) has `fontSize`/`fontFamily`/`fontStyle` and no `fontWeight` entry, so `apply.js:227-229` rejects it with *"Unsupported variable field"*; `variableFieldEnum` (`apply.ts:21-47`) omits it the same way one layer up. Neither layer discovered the truth because [BUG-030] accepted the false premise and shipped it as guidance.
- **Cost shape**: every weight binding in session 53 went through `run_script` — #70, #71, #75, #76, #77, #83, six calls one `edit` batch could have carried. The alias also actively misdirects: binding `fontStyle` is a *different* operation (a STRING face literal, not a family+weight lookup) and cannot express what a `font/weight/*` FLOAT token means.
- **Proposed fix**: add `fontWeight: "fontWeight"` to `FIELD_MAP`, add `"fontWeight"` to `variableFieldEnum`, delete `VARIABLE_FIELD_ALIASES.fontWeight` and the trailing parenthetical in the `variables` description. Ship with [BUG-034] — a bindable `fontWeight` that silently fails to resolve is worse than one that is refused.
- **Note**: capability + description fix, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Supersedes remedy (a) of [BUG-030]. Related: [BUG-034] (the binding is inert on remote), [TOOL-032], [AGENT-026].

- **Recurred (2nd)**: Session 55 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote) paid the cost twice — `run_script` #25 and #32 exist solely to bind `fontWeight` on item labels and input values, work `edit` should have absorbed into the batches at #23 and #31. The [BUG-030] schema fix that shipped 2026-09-01 (`040ef9e`) makes the rejection readable but **still states this entry's refuted premise** in `VARIABLE_FIELD_ALIASES` (`apply.ts:44-47`). Closing it needs all three: add `fontWeight` to `variableFieldEnum` (`apply.ts:20-37`), add the `FIELD_MAP` entry (`src/figma_plugin/src/commands/styles.js:1166-1168`), and delete the alias.
- **Recurred (3rd session) — and the same gap exists on the `run_script` surface**: Session 56 (2026-09-01, same file) #26 assembled the entire 6-variant `Combobox Item` set (~40 nodes, 12 bindings) and was rejected wholesale by **`fig.bindVariable`**, not `edit`: `Unsupported variable field: fontWeight. Fix: use one of: … fontSize, fontFamily, fontStyle, lineHeight, letterSpacing …`. The agent worked around it in #27 with a raw `node.setBoundVariable('fontWeight', v)`, which Figma accepted — a 4th independent proof of this entry's premise. **This matters for the fix's scope**: `fig.bindVariable` and `apply.js` share `FIELD_MAP` (`src/figma_plugin/src/commands/styles.js:1166-1168`), so adding `fontWeight` there repairs both surfaces, whereas a fix scoped to `apply.ts`'s `variableFieldEnum` alone would leave the script path broken. Add ~2 calls/session to the estimate for the script surface.

- **Session 62 locates a surface where it already works, and confirms the other two still do not (2026-09-02, same file, remote)**: `bindVariablesToStyle` (`src/figma_plugin/src/commands/styles.js:811-815`) passes any non-`color` field straight to `style.setBoundVariable(field, variable)` without consulting `FIELD_MAP`, and the MCP schema is an unconstrained `z.record(z.string(), z.string())` (`src/figmagent_mcp/tools/tokens.ts:719-724`) — so **`create_styles`/`update_styles` can bind `fontWeight` on a text style today**. Only the description omits it. Meanwhile `FIELD_MAP` (`styles.js:1166-1172`) and `variableFieldEnum` (`src/figmagent_mcp/tools/apply.ts:30-37`) still lack the entry and `VARIABLE_FIELD_ALIASES.fontWeight` (`apply.ts:43-45`) still asserts the refuted premise, so `edit` and `fig.bindVariable` remain blocked — verified against current source as of this session. **Fifth independent proof of the premise**: session 62's #6, #8 and #9 called `setBoundVariable('fontWeight', …)` on nine text styles and Figma accepted every one. This changes the fix's shape slightly: the `create_styles` path needs only a description change ([TOOL-050]), while `edit`/`fig.bindVariable` need the three-part code fix already specified here.

### [BUG-034] A `fontWeight` binding made from the remote VM is inert — bound but unresolved, and reported as success

- **Status**: identified
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: the family-toggle re-resolution is the last restore step in park-and-restore; a still-mismatched weight warns `weight_unresolved` naming the plugin transport. Recorded on [#158](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/158).
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: plugin-bug
- **Auto-fixable**: no (depends on BUG-033's park-and-restore)
- **First seen**: Session 53 second half (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 53
- **Estimated savings**: ~7 calls/session on custom-font files; removes a class of silently-wrong design output
- **Description**: Figma's `fontName` is `{family, style}` — only `family` is variable-backed; `style` is a plain literal. Binding `fontWeight` does not store a number, it runs a **family + weight → face lookup** and rewrites that literal, and the lookup only fires on a binding-change event *in a client that can enumerate the family's faces*. The headless `use_figma` VM cannot when the family is absent ([BUG-033]), so it **silently no-ops** and the node keeps the authoring font's face-name spelling. Inter spells 600 `Semi Bold`; PP Neue Montreal spells it `Semibold` — so nodes authored on Inter land on a face the real family does not have, render at 400, and show the weight variable struck through in the Figma UI.
- **The measurement (#84)**: 3 of 8 labels read `PP Neue Montreal Semibold | w=600`; **5 of 8 read `PP Neue Montreal Semi Bold | w=400` while bound to the 600 variable.** The node contradicts itself — `boundVariables.fontWeight` points at semibold, `fontWeight` returns 400 — and nothing in any response says so. #70 returned `failed: []` with all eight listed as bound.
- **The control (#71)**: four unbind/rebind cycles on one node, `attempts: []` (zero exceptions), face unchanged, `bound: true`. A silent no-op wearing a success shape — the cleanest instance of this failure class in 53 sessions.
- **Verified one-call workaround (#83)**: the lookup fires on a *family* change. Bind `fontFamily` to a different family variable, then back — `before: Semibold/600` → `toSerif: PP Editorial Old Semibold/600` → `toSans: PP Neue Montreal Semibold/600`. Worked first try.
- **Three rebuild attempts failed on three different causes before this was found** (#75 `insertChild` on a missing-font node; #76 raw `setBoundVariable('fills', …)` → *"fills and strokes variable bindings must be set on paints directly"*, needing the stdlib's `fig.bindVariable`, which the `run_script` description does not mention; #77 its own rollback guard firing on `Regular/400 ×6`).
- **Proposed fix**: after a `fontWeight` bind, compare `node.fontWeight` against the variable's resolved value; on a mismatch either apply the family-toggle re-resolution automatically or `fail()` with it as the stated fix. A bind that did not take must never ride an `is_error: false` response — same rule as [BUG-033](c).
- **Note**: plugin-bug, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [TOOL-037] (the field is refused by `edit` in the first place), [BUG-033] (the font absence underneath), [BUG-035] (the face-name spelling), [TOOL-033] (`run_script` has no assertion layer to catch it).

### [BUG-035] The weight → face table is Inter-spelled, duplicated in three places, and misses silently

- **Status**: identified
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: one face table in helpers.js; on a miss enumerate the family's real faces and pick by proximity, else fail with the actual face names. `font_fallback` compares the face, not just the family. Recorded on [#159](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/159).
- **Priority**: P2
- **Category**: plugin-bug
- **Auto-fixable**: no (code consolidation across three files)
- **First seen**: Session 53 second half (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 17 (as [BUG-007]), 53, 55, 58
- **Estimated savings**: ~3 calls/session, and one class of silently-wrong font output on both transports
- **Description**: The numeric-weight → face-name map exists three times — `src/figma_plugin/src/helpers.js:122-132` (`FONT_WEIGHT_STYLES`, also exported to `run_script` as `fig.loadFont`), a local duplicate at `src/figma_plugin/src/commands/apply.js:838-848`, and `create.js:106` importing the first — and every spelling in it is **Inter's**: `600: "Semi Bold"`, `700: "Bold"`, `800: "Extra Bold"`. PP Neue Montreal uses `Semibold`/`Extrabold`; other families use `SemiBold` or `DemiBold`. On a miss, `loadFontWithFallback` (`helpers.js:146-152`) catches and returns **Inter Regular** with no warning, and `create.js:108-123` does the same, so the write reports success on the wrong font.
- **Why the existing assertion does not catch it**: `font_fallback` (`assertions.js:155-173`) compares `fontName.family` only (`:162`), so a wrong **face inside the right family** never warns — and `run_script`, where all of session 53's writes happened, has no assertion layer at all ([TOOL-033]).
- **Relationship to [BUG-007]**: that entry is marked `implemented (bda7a09)` and its description names this exact defect (*"weight style name mismatches (e.g. 'Semi Bold' vs 'SemiBold') are swallowed"*). The `create` **symptom** was fixed; the shared table underneath it was not, and two more copies have appeared since. Treat as the surviving root cause rather than a reopen.
- **Proposed fix**: collapse the three tables to one, and on a miss enumerate the family's real faces via `listAvailableFontsAsync` and pick by proximity before falling back — `fail()`ing with the family's actual face names rather than silently landing on Inter. The agent reached the same conclusion independently and wrote it to project memory: *"never let a numeric weight choose a face."*
- **Note**: plugin-bug + de-duplication, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [BUG-007] (the fixed symptom), [BUG-034] (the spelling is what makes the inert binding invisible), [BUG-033].

- **Session 55 sharpens the requirement**: the donor face must match the target on **style-name spelling**, not on numeric weight. #85 picked `SF Pro / Semibold` precisely because "Semibold" is spelled the way `PP Neue Montreal`'s face is, and the `fontFamily` re-bind then carried the style axis across intact. A weight→face table keyed on `600 → "Semi Bold"` (Inter's spelling) would have produced `PP Neue Montreal / Semi Bold`, which does not exist. Any fix to the table needs the *file's own* style names as the key space, read from the text styles, not a hard-coded numeric map.
- **Corroborated (session 58)**: the session never let a numeric weight pick a face — it named `Semibold` explicitly throughout — and #3's font dump makes the hazard this entry describes directly visible: `Inter` offers `Semi Bold` (two words) while `Noto Looped Thai`, `Roboto` and `Noto Sans New Tai Lue` offer `SemiBold`/`Semibold`, and only five families in the whole 1,938-family VM inventory carry the one-word spelling. A `600 → "Semi Bold"` table is Inter-correct and wrong for every other family in that list.

### [AGENT-032] Put text on text styles, not per-node font properties — styles self-heal, nodes do not

- **Status**: identified
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: agent-behavior
- **First seen**: Session 53 second half (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 53
- **Estimated savings**: converts an N-node manual-repair problem into a 12-place one Figma fixes itself
- **Description**: One `run_script` call (#84) measured both halves at once. The 12 text styles created from the remote VM on Inter faces had **re-resolved themselves to correct faces** — `body/3 | PP Neue Montreal Semibold`, `heading/1 | PP Neue Montreal Extrabold` — because a client that has the font re-fires the face lookup when the style is opened. In the same call, **5 of 8 individual TEXT nodes were still stuck** on `Semi Bold | w=400` ([BUG-034]). Face correctness on styles is self-repairing; on nodes it needs a manual re-pick per node, in the Figma UI, forever.
- **Rule**: on the remote transport, bind font primitives once on a **text style** and apply the style; do not set `fontFamily`/`fontWeight`/`fontStyle` per node. This also routes the work through `create_styles`/`update_styles` ([AGENT-026]) instead of `run_script`, and through a path that loads fonts explicitly rather than through a bare `catch` ([BUG-032]).
- **User confirmation**: reached independently at #85 — *"let's stick with the remote transport and use the type styles that apply properties correctly, that's what we should be doing anyways."*
- **Note**: agent-behavior / documentation change (CLAUDE.md "Figma Design Patterns" + `edit`/`create_styles` descriptions), not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [BUG-034], [BUG-033], [AGENT-026], [BUG-032].

### [BUG-036] `combine_as_variants` has no page co-location pre-check — Figma's raw error escapes with no stated fix

- **Status**: planned
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: pre-check every component's page against the parent's; fail naming the component and both pages; with no parentId use the working page (BUG-047). Recorded on [#161](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/161).
- **Priority**: P2
- **Category**: plugin-bug
- **Auto-fixable**: yes (boundary-guard)
- **First seen**: Session 54 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 54
- **Estimated savings**: ~2-3 calls per occurrence, and closes a "no stated fix" hole in a function that already states three
- **Description**: `combine_as_variants({componentIds: ["23:3","24:2"], parentId: "3:678"})` (#32) failed with Figma's own text verbatim — `Error: in combineAsVariants: Grouped nodes must be in the same page as the parent` — no fix, no indication of *which* component was on the wrong page. `combineAsVariants` (`src/figma_plugin/src/commands/components.js:34-76`) pre-checks node existence (`:44-48`), COMPONENT type (`:49-53`) and `Property=Value` naming (`:60-66`), each with a proper `fail(message, fix)`; page co-location is the one precondition it does not check, so `figma.combineAsVariants` at `:76` throws raw.
- **Cost shape**: 2 calls of pure diagnosis. #33 was a script written solely to print `figma.currentPage`, `a.parent`, `b.parent` — returning `current: "0:1 Cover", aParent: "3:678 PAGE", bParent: "0:1 PAGE"` — and #34 re-did the combine after an `appendChild`. Every fact in #33's output is available inside the handler before it throws.
- **Proposed fix**: before `figma.combineAsVariants`, compare `pageOf(component)` against `pageOf(parent)` for each component and `fail()` naming the offender and both pages — fix: *"move it first with `write({ fromNodeId, parentId })`, or pass a `parentId` on the component's own page."* `pageOf` already exists as a shared helper (added for [BUG-018]).
- **Note**: error-handling / pre-check fix, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [AGENT-033] (what put the node on the wrong page), [BUG-018] (the same remote `currentPage` root condition at a different call site), [BUG-025] (raw Figma errors with no stated fix).

### [BUG-037] `edit`'s unloadable-font remedy tells the agent to abandon `textStyleId` — the style-face swap keeps it, and works

- **Status**: identified
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: `edit({textStyleId})` parks nodes (not the style) and applies internally; the 'skip textStyleId' remedy text is retired once parking lands. See #145. Recorded on [#162](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/162).
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: plugin-bug
- **Auto-fixable**: no (covered by BUG-033's park-and-restore)
- **First seen**: Session 54 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 54, 55, 57, 59
- **Estimated savings**: ~4 calls/session on custom-font files, and retires guidance that contradicts [AGENT-032]
- **Description**: [BUG-032]'s fix has shipped (`src/figma_plugin/src/commands/apply.js:328-336`). When a text style resolves but its own font cannot load, `failStyleLookup` now states the true cause — correct — and then prescribes: *"apply a text style whose font is available, or **skip `textStyleId`** and set the type directly with `edit` (`fontFamily` naming an available family, plus `fontWeight`/`fontSize`), then re-bind the `fontFamily` variable last."* That remedy **breaks the style link and moves the type onto per-node font properties**, which is exactly what [AGENT-032] was filed one session earlier to forbid: *"styles self-heal, nodes do not."* Two entries written from the same file 48 hours apart currently give opposite advice, and the one that ships in an error message is the wrong one.
- **The better remedy, proven in-session (#68)**: swap the **style's own** `fontName` onto an available family, apply the style to the nodes, then re-bind the style's `fontFamily` variable. Measured result — `before.face: "PP Neue Montreal/Regular"` → `applied: ["23:5 style=set", "24:4 style=set"]` → `after.face: "PP Neue Montreal/Regular"` with `boundVariables` (`fontSize`, `fontFamily`, `fontWeight`) intact. **The style link survives, the real family comes back, and every node on that style is fixed in one operation** rather than N. This is the style-level counterpart of [BUG-033]'s verified node-level swap-write-rebind, and it strictly dominates it.
- **Why the shipped text is worse than no advice**: on a design-system file the styles are the point. Session 54 spent #39 (soft-failed on 8 nodes), #41 (`edit` hard-failed on 2), #67 (soft-failed on 2) and #68 (the swap) discovering by hand what the message could have stated once.
- **Proposed fix**: (a) rewrite the `kind === "font"` branch of `failStyleLookup` to prescribe the style-face swap — *"the style's own font is absent from this VM; set the style's `fontFamily`/`fontStyle` to an available family with `update_styles`, apply the style, then re-bind the style's `fontFamily` variable — the binding survives even when the face cannot render"*; (b) better still, have `edit` perform the swap-apply-rebind internally when the style carries a `fontFamily` variable binding, so `edit({textStyleId})` simply succeeds; (c) mirror the wording into the `edit` and `update_styles` descriptions and the CLAUDE.md "Custom fonts are absent on the remote transport" paragraph, which repeats the same "skip `textStyleId`" advice.
- **Note**: error-message + optional capability fix, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [BUG-032] (the message this corrects), [AGENT-032] (which it contradicts), [BUG-033] (the font absence underneath), [BUG-034].

- **Corroborated independently**: Session 55 #85 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote) ran the style-face swap on two group labels and measured the result — authoring on `SF Pro / Semibold` then re-binding the `fontFamily` variable last returned `{"family":"PP Neue Montreal","style":"Semibold"}` on both nodes, and the page-wide audit (#86) found all 35 TEXT nodes resolved to the real family. The swap keeps the face **and** the style axis, which is the stronger claim this entry makes against `edit`'s shipped remedy.

- **Recurred (4th) — the recipe works and is LOSSY, now tracked as [BUG-044]**: Session 59 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote). #15 ran the style-level swap on `support/label/1` and applied it to three TEXT nodes (`nodeStyleApplied: [true, true, true]`) — 4-for-4 on the thing this entry claims. But the style went in on `PP Neue Montreal / Semibold` with `boundVariables` `{fontSize, fontFamily, fontWeight}` and came back `PP Neue Montreal / **Regular**` with `styleBound: ["fontFamily", "fontSize"]`: **assigning `fontName` drops an existing `fontWeight` binding, and the family re-bind does not restore the face**. Two further scripts (#16 detect, #17 repair) were needed, and the repair required assigning `fontName` *twice* because binding `fontWeight` rewrites the face literal. Session 57 restored `fontWeight`/`lineHeight`/`letterSpacing` explicitly and never saw this; session 59 followed the shorter recipe **as this entry documents it** and corrupted a style shared with `Accordion` and every other component in the file. **Fix (b) — have `edit` perform the swap-apply-rebind internally — is now the load-bearing half of this entry**, because the snapshot-and-restore this needs is exactly what a script author will keep omitting. Fix (a)'s wording must gain the weight/face/lineHeight restore before it is copied anywhere else. Details and the repair sequence: [BUG-044].


- **Corroborated at document scale (session 62, 2026-09-02, same file, remote)** — the strongest confirmation to date, and the first covering a whole document rather than one component. Three park-and-swap scripts applied text styles across 15 component pages: #17 (Alert Dialog, 2 layers), #18 (six pages, styles derived from each component's `@include type-style(...)` in SCSS), #23 (chip and group-label corrections). Each snapshots every affected style's bindings and metrics, parks **both** the target styles and the source styles the nodes already carry (a node on a PP-based style cannot take a `fontName` write either), applies, then restores metrics → weight → size → **family last**. Final verification #24 returned `unstyledCount: 1` (the Cover wordmark, correctly out of scope) and `styleIntegrityIssues: []` — zero styles left with wrong face or missing bindings. **The session also produced the negative control**: #12 attempted the same retarget with a bare `setTextStyleIdAsync` and no park, and threw `Cannot set style successfully: unloaded font "PP Neue Montreal Regular"`. Park present → 3 for 3; park absent → fails.
- **New call site for the misreported-remedy half**: #12's error told the agent to call `figma.loadFontAsync({family: "PP Neue Montreal", style: "Regular"})` — the one call that cannot succeed in that VM. The agent correctly ignored it ([AGENT-029]) and asked the user instead. [BUG-033](c) proposes detecting family absence via `listAvailableFontsAsync` and stating the true remedy; that fix needs to cover `setTextStyleIdAsync` **inside `run_script`**, which neither [BUG-033] nor this entry currently names as a site.

### [TOOL-038] No first-class variable-mode override — checking a design in its second mode costs 6 calls

- **Status**: identified
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: both: `edit.variableModes` ({ collection: mode | null }, names or ids) and `screenshot({ variableModes })` as a set-export-clear override in one call. Recorded on [#163](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/163).
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: missing-tool
- **Auto-fixable**: no (missing-tool: variableModes on edit/screenshot)
- **First seen**: Session 54 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 54
- **Estimated savings**: ~4 calls per themed-review session; the operation is currently unreachable without `run_script`
- **Description**: `setExplicitVariableModeForCollection` and `clearExplicitVariableModeForCollection` appear **nowhere** in the repository — not in `apply.ts`, not in `apply.js`, not in the `fig.*` stdlib. Looking at a component in dark mode, the single most routine review action on a two-mode design system, is therefore remote-only and hand-written. Session 54 paid 6 calls for one dark-mode look: #57 set the mode, #58 screenshotted, #59 diagnosed why it looked wrong, #60 repaired it, #61 re-screenshotted, #62 cleared the override and tidied.
- **Compounding trap found in the same sequence**: the scene was built with `figma.createComponent()` (#38) and the agent's `paintVar` helper bound a variable onto the node's **existing** paint, inheriting its `visible: false`. `#59` read back `sceneFills: [{"visible": false, …, "boundVariables": {"color": …}}]` — a correctly bound colour that can never render. `write`/`edit` have a `fill_not_applied` assertion for this shape; `run_script` has none ([TOOL-033]).
- **Proposed fix**: add `variableModes` to `edit` — `{ "<collectionId>": "<modeId>" }` to set, `null` to clear — mapping to `setExplicitVariableModeForCollection` / `clearExplicitVariableModeForCollection`. Accept collection *names* as well as IDs, since `get_design_system` already returns both. A two-line addition to the `FIELD_MAP`-adjacent handling in `apply.js` plus a Zod field in `apply.ts`.
- **Note**: missing-tool capability gap — not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist; no auto-plan generated. Related: [TOOL-033] (the invisible-paint assertion that would have saved 3 of the 6 calls), [AGENT-025].

- **Recurred (2nd) — the irreducible overhead is 2 calls, and it is now isolated**: Session 57 (2026-09-01, same file) checked dark mode in **3** calls against session 54's 6: #32 `setExplicitVariableModeForCollection(col, '2:0')`, #33 the screenshot, #34 `clearExplicitVariableModeForCollection`. The halving is entirely because this agent avoided session 54's invisible-paint trap (see [BUG-042], which is the lint-side face of the same `visible: false` paint) — the mode-override ceremony itself did not get cheaper. **2 of 3 calls are pure overhead, paid on every themed review of a two-mode file**, which is the routine case in this project. The agent did clear the override afterwards; a session that forgets leaves the page pinned to the wrong mode for everyone.

### [TOOL-039] `edit` can apply an `effectStyleId` but cannot set `effects` — a variable-bound shadow needs `run_script`

- **Status**: identified
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: `effects` array on edit and the write spec; `variables.effectColor` binds every shadow's colour via setBoundVariableForEffect; effectStyleId unchanged. Recorded on [#164](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/164).
- **Priority**: P2
- **Category**: missing-tool
- **Auto-fixable**: no (missing-tool: effects on edit)
- **First seen**: Session 54 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 54, 55, 61
- **Estimated savings**: ~1-2 calls per component with elevation; removes one more reason to open a script
- **Description**: `apply.ts` exposes `effectStyleId` (`:236`) and nothing else in the effects family — no `effects` array, no way to bind a variable to an effect colour. A dialog with `box-shadow: … var(--color-shadow-default)` therefore cannot be built with `edit` at all. Session 54 #31 wrote it by hand: `P.effects = [figma.variables.setBoundVariableForEffect(P.effects[0], "color", v)]`. The same call also had to hand-roll the focus ring, because `setBoundVariableForPaint` on `strokes` is likewise script-only (a known adjacent gap — see the `fig.bindVariable` stroke defect).
- **Proposed fix**: accept an `effects` array on `edit` (DROP_SHADOW / INNER_SHADOW / BLUR with the usual fields), and allow `variables: { effectColor: "VariableID:…" }` to route through `setBoundVariableForEffect`. Design-system files bind shadow colour as a matter of course, so the variable path is the point, not a nicety.
- **Note**: missing-tool capability gap — not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist; no auto-plan generated. Related: [TOOL-032] (`write` cannot bind variables at create time — same shape), [TOOL-027], [AGENT-025].

- **Recurred (2nd), and it is now four calls, not one**: Session 55 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote) needed `run_script` for the popup elevation **four separate times** — #46 (add the shadow, colour bound via `setBoundVariableForEffect`), #63 (rewrite it with `showShadowBehindNode` explicit), #68 (clear it to test an export), #73 (restore it). Every one is a one-line `n.effects = [...]` that `edit` cannot express. The diagnostic pair (#68/#73) is what isolated [BUG-016]'s discriminator, so an `effects` field on `edit` would also have made that experiment a two-call check instead of a script round trip.
### [AGENT-033] A raw `clone()` in `run_script` parents to `figma.currentPage`, not to the source's parent

- **Status**: planned
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: no `fig.clone` helper; `write({ fromNodeId, parentId })` is the path; once BUG-047 lands a raw clone() parents to the session's own page. Recorded on [#165](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/165).
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: agent-behavior
- **Auto-fixable**: yes (description-only)
- **First seen**: Session 54 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 54
- **Estimated savings**: ~3 calls per occurrence, plus a class of silently-misplaced nodes that no assertion catches
- **Description**: Session 54 #31 cloned a COMPONENT inside a script — `const F = P.clone(); F.x = 0; F.y = P.height + 80;` — positioning it as though it sat beside its source. It did not. Figma's `clone()` appends to `figma.currentPage`, and on the remote transport `currentPage` is whatever page the file was last left on ([BUG-018], confirmed session 52: `currentPage` does not persist between remote calls). #33 measured the damage: `current: "0:1 Cover", aParent: "3:678 PAGE", bParent: "0:1 PAGE"`. The clone was on the Cover page with plausible-looking coordinates, and nothing said so — the failure only surfaced two calls later when `combine_as_variants` refused it ([BUG-036]).
- **The tell**: every other write script in the session opened with a `getNodeByIdAsync → loadAsync → setCurrentPageAsync` preamble. **#31 is the only one that did not**, and it is the only one that broke. The preamble is load-bearing and copy-pasted, which is its own argument for [TOOL-023].
- **The first-class path is already correct**: `cloneNode` (`src/figma_plugin/src/commands/modify.js:487-491`) does `node.parent.appendChild(clone)` and `cloneAndModify` (`:518-521`) honours an explicit `parentId`, so `write({ fromNodeId: "23:3", parentId: "3:678" })` is correct by construction. This is a concrete, measured cost of the [AGENT-025] monoculture: the guard existed, in this repo, and the session routed around it.
- **Proposed fix**: (a) agent guidance in the `run_script` description — *"`clone()` appends to `figma.currentPage`, which on remote is not the page you are writing to. `appendChild` to an explicit parent immediately after cloning, or use `write({ fromNodeId, parentId })`."*; (b) add a `fig.clone(nodeId, parentId)` stdlib helper that mirrors `cloneNode`'s parenting, so the escape hatch inherits the guard rather than re-deriving it.
- **Note**: agent-behavior + stdlib addition, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [BUG-036] (the failure it caused), [BUG-018] (the same `currentPage` root condition), [TOOL-023], [AGENT-025].

### [BUG-038] An `opacity` variable binding resolves at 1/100 scale — the node renders invisible and nothing warns

- **Status**: identified
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: prepare_figma_variables scales opacity tokens ×100 when ≤ 1 and lists them; post-bind `opacity_scale` assertion warns when resolved < 0.05 and variable > 0.05; no rewrite at bind time. Recorded on [#166](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/166).
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: plugin-bug
- **Auto-fixable**: no (mixed: prepare_figma_variables conversion + assertion + description)
- **First seen**: Session 55 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 55, 57, 58, 61
- **Estimated savings**: ~4 calls per occurrence, and it converts a silent visual defect into a stated warning
- **Description**: `edit`'s `variables` map accepts `opacity` (`apply.ts:20-37`), so this fires through the **first-class tool**, not only through `run_script`. Session 55 #21 bound `opacity` on a `State=Disabled` variant to `VariableID:2:492`; the variant went invisible. #71 measured the node in the VM — `{"opacity": 0.004000000189989805, "bound": {… "opacity": {"type":"VARIABLE_ALIAS","id":"VariableID:2:492"} …}}` — and #72 read the variable's own value: `{"2:2": 0.4000000059604645}`. **The variable holds 0.4; the resolved node opacity is 0.004.** Figma resolves a FLOAT variable bound to `opacity` on a 0-100 percentage scale, so a token authored on the CSS 0-1 scale — which is what every DTCG/Style-Dictionary pipeline emits — renders at 0.4%.
- **Nothing at any layer catches it**: `edit` accepts the binding, the write response carries no warning, the post-write assertions do not check it, and `lint` (#81/#82) does not flag it. The agent found it only by screenshotting the variant (#70) and seeing nothing, then spending three calls (#70, #71, #72) to measure the cause and one (#73) to repair it by unbinding and setting `opacity = 0.4` literally.
- **Proposed fix**: add a post-write assertion in `src/figma_plugin/src/assertions.js` — after an `opacity` variable bind, if the node's resolved `opacity` is `< 0.05` while the bound variable's value is `> 0.05`, warn with the fix (bind a 0-100-scaled variable, or set `opacity` directly). Same shape as the existing balloon-frame and FILL-not-applied assertions. Document the scale in `edit`'s `variables` description in the same change.
- **Note**: the agent's own in-script comment states it exactly — *"Figma reads opacity variables as 0-100; the pipeline emits 0-1, so 0.4 binds as 0.4%."* Suspected to generalise to `lineHeight`/`letterSpacing`, which have the same unit ambiguity; not measured here. Assertion + description fix, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [TOOL-025], [BUG-034] (a binding that reports success without resolving), [AGENT-024].

- **Recurred (2nd) — and the workaround leaves permanent, unresolvable `lint` debt**: Session 57 (2026-09-01, same file). #17 bound `opacity` on all three `State=Disabled` variants to `checkbox/disabled-opacity`; screenshot #18 showed them rendering invisibly; #22 unbound all three and set `opacity = 0.4` literally. **Discovery cost fell from 4 calls to 1** — the agent had the quirk in project memory and recognised the symptom on sight, which is the entry working as documentation. The new fact is what happens next: `lint` #29 returned `opacity: 3` among its 20 issues, `near_match`, pointing at the very variable that cannot be bound. **The only working repair for this bug creates a lint finding that must be ignored on every subsequent run, forever**, and the closing summary had to carve it out by hand ("the documented literal opacity"). The cost is therefore not the one-time 4 calls this entry records but a standing tax on every future `lint` of the component. Strengthens the case for the proposed assertion, and adds a second remedy: `lint` should not report an `opacity` `near_match` against a variable whose value is on the 0-1 scale, since binding it is known-wrong.
- **Recurred (3rd)**: Session 58 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote) carried the literal `opacity: 0.4` on all 12 Disabled variants deliberately rather than binding the variable, and annotated the reason. `lint` reported those 12 as unbound findings the agent triaged and left. Same permanent-lint-debt outcome as session 57 — the workaround is now being applied prophylactically at build time, which means the cost has moved from discovery calls into standing false positives in every `lint` run on these files.

- **Recurred (4th) — the literal-`0.4` workaround is now standing lint debt on a second component**: Session 61 (2026-09-02, external storybook/"Archer", remote). All 5 `State=Disabled` variants of `Context Menu Item` plus 2 disabled items inside `Content=Disabled` carry `opacity: 0.4000000059604645` as a raw literal — **7 of the session's 21 lint findings, every one an `exact_match` that can never be safely auto-fixed**, because binding the variable makes the node render at 0.4% and vanish. The agent knew this from project memory and stated it in its closing summary. Each session that ships a disabled state on this file adds permanent, un-fixable lint noise; combined with [TOOL-049]'s set-wrapper chrome, the *actionable* finding count for this build was zero out of 21.

### [BUG-039] `setProperties` failures state a fix for the wrong problem when the real cause is an unavailable font

- **Status**: planned
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: branch the `setProperties` catch on the unloaded-font shape and route into park-and-restore (the main component's TEXT nodes are what get parked). Recorded on [#167](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/167).
- **Priority**: P2
- **Category**: plugin-bug
- **Auto-fixable**: yes (boundary-guard)
- **First seen**: Session 55 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 55
- **Estimated savings**: ~2-3 calls per occurrence on any custom-font file
- **Description**: `src/figma_plugin/src/commands/apply.js:675-683` attaches one unconditional fix string to **every** `setProperties` throw: *"verify each value matches its property type — VARIANT options must be exact, INSTANCE_SWAP needs a valid COMPONENT id; read the instance to list valid keys and options"*. Session 55 #44 batched 17 ops; 8 failed with `setProperties failed on instance 27:18: in setProperties: Unable to update this text property because the component uses a font that isn't available.` followed by that same property-type advice. Figma's own message names the cause exactly; the appended fix points at an unrelated one and would cost 2-3 calls to disprove.
- **Same defect as [BUG-032], in a different function**: stating a cause you did not measure is the same failure as stating no fix at all. [BUG-032] was fixed by recording *which* await threw and branching the remedy; `setProperties` never got the same treatment.
- **Proposed fix**: branch on the caught message. When it matches the font-unavailable shape (`/font.*(isn't|is not|not) available|unloaded font/`), state the remedy the project already documents — swap the component's TEXT nodes to an available face, set the properties, then re-bind the `fontFamily` variable last ([BUG-037]'s style-face swap). Keep the generic property-type text for everything else.
- **Agent recovery was good**: it read past the wrong fix, recognised the font cause and pivoted to `run_script` with an inline font swap on the very next call (#45) — one call, no permutation storm. [AGENT-029] executed correctly; the message is still wrong.
- **Note**: error-handling fix, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [BUG-032] (same shape, already fixed), [BUG-033] (the underlying font failure), [BUG-008].
- **Recurred (2nd) — second session, second component, and the retry is now more expensive**: Session 56 (2026-09-01, same file). #57 and #60 both assembled the `Combobox Popup` set by instantiating `Combobox Item` and calling `setProperties` for each label; both failed with `in setProperties: Unable to update this text property because the component uses a font that isn't available.` followed by the same unconditional property-type advice. Because the failure sat inside a 90-line atomic assembly script, each rejection discarded the **whole set**, and the agent spent #58 and #59 re-deriving the remedy this tracker already contains (park the *main component's* labels on a loadable face first). Recovery took 4 calls against session 55's 1 — the message cost more here precisely because the caller was a bigger script. Fix unchanged (`src/figma_plugin/src/commands/apply.js:675-683`, branch on `/font.*(isn't|is not|not) available|unloaded font/`).

### [TOOL-040] `fig.createNode` returns the create *response*, not the node — `.id` is undefined, and `fig.check` is skipped in silence

- **Status**: planned
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: `fig.createNode` returns the created `tree` object; the write-mode postlude warns on null/undefined ids instead of skipping silently. Ships with TOOL-033. Recorded on [#168](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/168).
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: missing-tool
- **Auto-fixable**: yes (boundary-guard)
- **First seen**: Session 55 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 55
- **Estimated savings**: ~3 calls per script-heavy session, plus it restores post-write assertions that are currently skipped without notice
- **Description**: `src/figma_plugin/src/remote_entries/stdlib.js:59` defines `createNode: (spec, parentId) => create({ tree: spec, parentId })`, and `create()` returns `{ success, totalNodesCreated, tree: { id, name, type, children } }` (`src/figma_plugin/src/commands/create.js:459-464`) — **no `id`, no `children` at the top level**. The `run_script` description (`src/figmagent_mcp/tools/script.ts:154`) calls it "the full write tree builder" and states no return shape, while documenting the returns of `fig.loadFont`, `fig.bindVariable` and `fig.check` on the lines around it.
- **It fired three ways in one session**: (a) **#40** walked the results — `roots.push(walk(n))` where `walk` reads `n.id`/`n.children` — and returned `{"nodeIds": [null, null, null], "roots": [{"children": []} x3]}` with `is_error: false`. The nodes were created, but the write-mode postlude (`script.ts:129`) then ran `fig.check([null, null, null])` inside a bare `try {} catch (_e) {}`, so **no post-write assertion ran and nothing said so**; the agent spent #41 (a 57-node `read` of the whole page) purely to discover the IDs it had just created, and #42 to delete an orphan the read surfaced. (b) **#55** wrote `const root = await figma.getNodeByIdAsync(typeof composite === 'string' ? composite : composite.id)` → `undefined` → `null` → `TypeError: cannot read property 'children' of null`, discarding an entire atomic write script; #56 was a full re-send. (c) **#56** defensively returned `createdShape: Object.keys(created)` and got back `["success","totalNodesCreated","tree"]` — the agent had to reverse-engineer the contract at runtime.
- **Proposed fix**: return the created node's `tree` object (`{ id, name, type, children }`) from `fig.createNode` — that is what every call site expects and it makes `walk()`-shaped code work unchanged. Separately, have the write-mode postlude report when `__result.nodeIds` contains `null`/`undefined` entries instead of swallowing it: an all-null id list is never intentional, and today it silently forfeits the warnings block.
- **Note**: stdlib contract + description fix, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [TOOL-033] (`run_script` returns no post-write assertions — this is a second path to the same hole), [BUG-026], [AGENT-025].

### [TOOL-041] `edit` cannot set `strokeAlign` or a node/component `description`

- **Status**: identified
- **Priority**: P2
- **Category**: missing-tool
- **First seen**: Session 55 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 55
- **Estimated savings**: ~2-3 calls per component-library session
- **Description**: Two small direct-value fields with no first-class path, both hit in one session. (a) **`strokeAlign`** — `document.js:343,364` reads it back into FSGN, but nothing writes it: no `strokeAlign` in `apply.ts` or `apply.js`. Session 55 folded `n.strokeAlign = 'INSIDE'` into `run_script` #25 and #32 because CSS borders are inside-aligned and Figma's default is not. (b) **`description`** — a COMPONENT's description is what shows in the assets panel and Dev Mode, and no tool sets it: `grep` finds no `description` write in `create.js`, `apply.js` or `components.js`. Session 55 #80 wrote descriptions for all four component sets by hand, mapping each to its source file and CSS classes.
- **Proposed fix**: add both as direct-value fields on `edit` — `strokeAlign` (`INSIDE`/`OUTSIDE`/`CENTER`, guarded to nodes with strokes) and `description` (guarded to COMPONENT/COMPONENT_SET). Both are additive, mechanical, and remove a `run_script` escape that currently forfeits the pre-checks, warnings and mini-lint a first-class write carries.
- **Note**: follows the precedent of [TOOL-025] and [TOOL-027] — small missing direct-value fields grouped into one entry. Not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist; no auto-plan generated. Related: [TOOL-035] (per-side stroke weight, same field family), [AGENT-025].

### [TOOL-042] Annotations can be written but never deleted — clearing one needs `run_script`

- **Status**: identified
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: `delete: true` on a set_annotation / set_multiple_annotations entry clears `annotationIndex` or all; `labelMarkdown` becomes optional; neither → fail with the fix. Recorded on [#170](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/170).
- **Priority**: P2
- **Category**: missing-tool
- **Auto-fixable**: no (missing-tool: a new delete verb on the annotation schema)
- **First seen**: Session 55 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 55
- **Estimated savings**: ~1-2 calls per annotation-revision pass
- **Description**: `labelMarkdown` is a required `z.string()` on both `set_annotation` (`src/figmagent_mcp/tools/comments.ts:221`) and `set_multiple_annotations` (`:272`), and neither tool has a delete/clear verb. There is therefore no first-class way to remove an annotation once written — only to overwrite it with different text. Session 55 wrote 10 annotations (#89), reviewed them against its own project memory, decided 2 only restated what the canvas already shows, and had to open `run_script` #91 (`n.annotations = []` on two nodes) to remove them before rewriting the remaining 8 (#92).
- **Proposed fix**: accept `delete: true` on a `set_annotation` / `set_multiple_annotations` entry in place of `labelMarkdown` (mirroring `edit`'s `delete: true` idiom), clearing the annotation at `annotationIndex` or all of them when the index is omitted. Make `labelMarkdown` optional and reject an entry carrying neither with a stated fix.
- **Note**: revising annotations down is the *right* behaviour for dev-mode documentation — an annotation that restates the canvas is noise — and the toolchain currently makes the correct move the expensive one. Missing-tool capability gap, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist; no auto-plan generated. Related: [TOOL-041], [AGENT-025].

### [TOOL-043] `lint` discards its whole issues array on a 5% budget overrun instead of trimming it

- **Status**: identified
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: paginate like grep: `page` on lint and get_design_system, whole items per page, `meta.pagination` with page/pageCount/next/total; read unchanged. Recorded on [#171](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/171).
- **Priority**: P2
- **Category**: tool-behavior
- **Auto-fixable**: no (behaviour change: pagination on lint/get_design_system)
- **First seen**: Session 55 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 55
- **Estimated savings**: ~1 call per lint pass, and it stops the agent under-shooting on the retry
- **Description**: Session 55 #81 ran `lint(nodeId: "3:679", maxIssues: 60)`; the plugin found 39 issues and serialised to **31,620 chars — 1,620 over** the 30,000 budget. `guardOutput` replaced the entire `issues` array with a summary and three narrowing hints, so a 5.4% overrun cost 100% of the detail. #82 retried at `maxIssues: 12`, got 12 of 39, and the remaining 27 were never seen.
- **`grep` already solved this shape**: an over-budget search paginates into budget-sized pages of whole groups and reports `meta.pagination`. `lint` computes every issue and then throws them all away.
- **Proposed fix**: at the `lint` call site (`src/figmagent_mcp/tools/lint.ts:95-105`), trim the `issues` array to what fits the budget and report `shown: N of M` alongside the existing summary and hints, rather than dropping all of them. `extractLintSummary` already knows the summary shape. Pagination (as `grep` has) is the fuller answer; trimming is the one-line version that recovers most of the value.
- **Note**: tool-behavior fix, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [TOOL-036] (`lint` reporting on a scan it skipped), [BUG-027].
- **Recurred (2nd) — the same defect measured on `read`, at a 1.2% overrun**: Session 56 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote) #14 ran `read("27:68", detail: "full", depth: 4, maxOutputChars: 28000)`; the serialiser produced **28,327 chars — 327 over** — and `guardOutput` returned the `meta` block plus four narrowing hints instead of the subtree. #15 re-ran the identical call at 32,000 and got it. **This is not a `lint` bug — it is a `guardOutput` bug**, so the proposed fix should move up a layer: trim to whole nodes/groups that fit and report `shown: N of M` at the guard, rather than patching each call site. Mitigating credit: the overrun message states the exact number to pass (`maxOutputChars: 29327`), which is why recovery cost one call and not a bisection — that hint is worth keeping in whatever replaces the discard.

### [BUG-040] A TEXT node on an unavailable font measures at width 0, and [BUG-009]'s documented repair does not work

- **Status**: planned
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: resize() is part of the restore step; `width_collapse` assertion on missing-font width 0; donors: Inter / Noto Sans New Tai Lue, SF families excluded; CLAUDE.md width-0 paragraph corrected. Recorded on [#172](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/172).
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: plugin-bug
- **Auto-fixable**: yes (assertion)
- **First seen**: Session 56 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 56, 58, 61 (**remedy verified**)
- **Estimated savings**: ~19 calls per custom-font session, and it corrects a remedy this tracker is actively prescribing
- **Description**: When a `fontFamily` variable bind (or `fontName` write) leaves a TEXT node on a family the remote VM cannot load ([BUG-033]), the node's **measured width goes to 0** and every sizing operation downstream is a silent no-op. Session 56 #35 is the control: two sibling labels in the same set, same characters, same parent, both `hasMissingFont: true` — `PP Neue Montreal/Regular` measures `w: 298`, `PP Neue Montreal/Semibold` measures `w: 0`. From there `layoutSizingHorizontal: FILL` (#30) returned `0, 0, 0`; a `primaryAxisSizingMode` AUTO→FIXED toggle plus a parent `resize(320)` (#36) returned `before: 0, mid: 0, after: 0`; hiding the sibling icon (#38) returned `0 → 0 → 0`. **Only an explicit `resize()` after unbinding and re-facing worked** (#39: `a: 0, b: 0, hug: 0, fixed: 275`). #45 is the negative control on `Inter` — the same construction on an available font measured 55, never 0 — so the missing font is the cause, not the FILL-before-resize order.
- **[BUG-009]'s two-pass recipe fails here, and CLAUDE.md teaches it**: the documented first step, `textAutoResize: "HEIGHT"`, **throws** on a missing-font node (#32: `Cannot write to node with unloaded font "PP Neue Montreal Semibold"`) rather than resizing it. CLAUDE.md's "Repairing a width-0 TEXT node" paragraph describes a different trigger (`WIDTH_AND_HEIGHT` under a constrained parent) and its remedy is unreachable in the font case.
- **The donor family decides whether it collapses — and the recommended donor is one of the bad ones**: #50 built the same missing `PP Neue Montreal/Semibold` node five times, varying only the family it was authored on before the rebind. `SF Pro` → `w: 0`; `SF Pro Rounded` → `w: 0`; `SF Compact` → `w: 0`; `SF Compact Rounded` → `w: 0`; `Noto Sans New Tai Lue` → `w: 228`. All five report `PP Neue Montreal/Semibold` and `renders: false` afterwards, but keep measuring on the **donor's** metrics. `SF Pro` is exactly the donor [BUG-033]/[BUG-035] adopted in session 55 (chosen because its style name spells "Semibold" correctly) — so the swap-write-rebind recipe as currently written produces the width-0 state unless an explicit `resize()` follows.
- **Proposed fix**: (a) post-write assertion — after any `fontName` write or `setBoundVariable("fontFamily", …)` that leaves `hasMissingFont: true`, check `width === 0` and emit `width_collapse` naming the font as the cause and `resize()` as the remedy (the assertion category already exists in `src/figma_plugin/src/assertions.js`); (b) amend the swap-write-rebind recipe in [BUG-033]/[BUG-035] and in CLAUDE.md to require an explicit `resize()` after the rebind, and to stop naming `SF Pro` as the donor without it; (c) correct CLAUDE.md's "Repairing a width-0 TEXT node" paragraph — `textAutoResize: HEIGHT` is not a valid first step when the node's font is missing.
- **Note**: assertion + documentation fix, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [BUG-009] (the FILL no-op this compounds), [BUG-033] (the font absence), [BUG-035] (the face/donor table), [TOOL-033] (`run_script` returns no assertions, so nothing warned).
- **Corroborated + REFRAMED (2nd) — the font does not have to be missing**: Session 58 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote) authored a label directly on `SF Pro / Semibold`: `figma.loadFontAsync` **resolved without throwing** (#2 would have aborted otherwise) and `n.fontName` read back as `{family:"SF Pro", style:"Semibold"}` — yet the node measured **`w: 0`** (#3). This is not a rebind off a missing family as in session 56; the face is present in `listAvailableFontsAsync`, loadable, and assigned, and still has no usable metrics. **This entry's framing — "a TEXT node on an *unavailable* font" — is therefore too narrow.** Presence in the VM's font list does not imply measurability.
- **The five-donor result reproduces exactly, and the donor list is now exhaustively explained**: #3 filtered the VM's 1,938 families for a one-word `Semibold` style and returned **exactly five** — `Noto Sans New Tai Lue`, `SF Compact`, `SF Compact Rounded`, `SF Pro`, `SF Pro Rounded` — precisely the five session 56 #50 tested, where four measured 0 and only `Noto Sans New Tai Lue` survived. Session 58 reproduces that on a different component, a different page and a different mechanism: `SF Pro` → `w: 0`, `Inter / Semi Bold` → `w: 46`, `Noto Sans New Tai Lue / Semibold` → `w: 51`, which is the face it shipped on. The list also explains the recurrence: a design system spelling its weight `Semibold` (as PP Neue Montreal does) can only land on one of those five, `Inter` is not among them (it spells the face `Semi Bold`), and four of the five are broken. **Grounds to change the recommended donor from `SF Pro` to `Noto Sans New Tai Lue` are now independent and threefold** (sessions 56, 57, 58).
- **[BUG-009]'s repair produced a false positive here**: #4 ran the documented `textAutoResize: 'NONE'` → `resize(60, 16)` → `'WIDTH_AND_HEIGHT'` recipe and returned `labelW: 60` — but 60 was a hardcoded guess, not a measurement. #5 (`characters` round-trip) and #6 (pure re-read) both confirmed the same supplied number. The label only measured honestly once the family changed. A repair verified against a value the agent itself wrote is not a repair; cf. [AGENT-034].

- **Remedy verified in production (session 61)** — the first build on this file to ship a semibold face with **zero** width-0 casualties. Script #27 loaded `{family:'Noto Sans New Tai Lue', style:'Semibold'}` as the donor, applied it to the group-label text, and reported `count: 17, semiboldFaces: 1` with every measured width non-zero (`w: 70`). This is sessions 56–58's correction to the `SF Pro` recommendation, carried forward via the project's `figma-remote-vm-gotchas.md` memory file and applied first try. The finding survives the round trip from tracker → memory → next session.

### [TOOL-044] `fig.prop` throws a raw `TypeError: invalid 'in' operand` on a null node, naming neither the node nor a fix

- **Status**: implemented — `fig.prop` fails with the argument echoed and the deleted/other-file remedy when the node is not an object (`remote_entries/stdlib.js`). Test in `tests/stdlib.test.ts`.
- **Priority**: P2
- **Category**: plugin-bug
- **First seen**: Session 56 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 56
- **Estimated savings**: ~1 call, and it removes an unattributable stack trace from the diagnostic path
- **Description**: Session 56 #43 looped `fig.prop(n, k)` over 31 keys for two node IDs. One of them (`42:67`) had been replaced at #37, so `figma.getNodeByIdAsync` returned `null`, and `fig.prop`'s `k in node` duck-type guard threw `TypeError: invalid 'in' operand at z (PLUGIN_133_SOURCE:1:532)`. The message names no node ID, no property and no fix — in a call whose entire purpose was to find out *which* node was broken. `fig.prop` exists specifically to be the safe read at serializer boundaries, so a raw `TypeError` from it is the one failure mode it is supposed to prevent.
- **Proposed fix**: in `src/figma_plugin/src/remote_entries/stdlib.js`, guard the node argument — when it is null/undefined or not an object, `fail()` with the argument echoed and the remedy: *"`getNodeByIdAsync` returned null — the node was deleted, or belongs to a different file than the one selected; check the return value before reading properties."* Same one-line shape as [BUG-024]'s fix. CLAUDE.md's rule ("no user-facing error without a stated fix") applies to the stdlib as much as to the command modules.
- **Note**: error-handling fix, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [TOOL-040] (`fig.createNode` returning the wrong object, same stdlib), [BUG-031] (`Node not found` for IDs the VM just returned), [BUG-024].

### [AGENT-034] Close out a build against a re-run of `lint`, not against the first run's summary buckets

- **Status**: identified
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: becomes figma-guidelines skill text (INFRA-008): re-run lint after a fix pass before reporting done; with pagination (TOOL-043) the uninspected-count half is moot. Recorded on [#174](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/174).
- **Priority**: P2
- **Category**: agent-behavior
- **Auto-fixable**: no (covered by INFRA-008's plan)
- **First seen**: Session 56 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 56
- **Estimated savings**: ~1 call, and it stops a class of unmeasured completion claim
- **Description**: Session 56 #76 ran `lint(nodeId: "5:684", maxIssues: 60)` and got `totalNodesScanned: 121, totalIssues: 93` with `byProperty: {fills: 50, itemSpacing: 7, opacity: 6, cornerRadius: 5, padding*: 20}` and `bySeverity: {exact_match: 6, near_match: 47, ambiguous: 40}` — **93 issues under a 60 cap, so 33 were never returned**. #77 then cleared 20 stray icon fills via `run_script`, and the session closed asserting all six components were "bound to `combobox/*` variables", characterising the remainder as "set-wrapper chrome and deliberate literal opacities" from the summary buckets alone. The triage is plausible and may well be right; nothing in the session measures it, and the largest bucket (`fills: 50`) is not the one the closing summary describes.
- **Why it matters here specifically**: the same session had *six* `exact_match` issues — the severity `autoFix` binds without ambiguity — and left them unfixed and unmentioned. A second `lint` after #77 would have cost one call and either confirmed the triage or surfaced the six.
- **Proposed fix**: agent guidance — after a fix pass triggered by `lint`, re-run `lint` before reporting the build complete, and when `totalIssues > maxIssues`, either raise the cap or state explicitly that N issues were never inspected. Pairs with [TOOL-043]: trimming instead of discarding would make the second pass cheaper, and [TOOL-036]'s "the structured verdict must match the prose" is the tool-side half of the same problem.
- **Note**: agent-behavior change, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [TOOL-043], [TOOL-036], [AGENT-019], [AGENT-016] (the opposite failure — this is under-verification, that was over-verification).

- **Recurred (2nd) — verbatim, one session later**: Session 57 (2026-09-01, same file). #29 ran `lint(["41:26", "42:42"])` → `totalNodesScanned: 43, totalIssues: 20, bySeverity: {exact_match: 3, near_match: 17}`. #31 then cleared the 6 phantom `fills` ([BUG-042]) and **`lint` was never run again**; the session closed with "Lint's remaining hits are the set/scene 40px chrome and the documented literal opacity" — a triage of the first run's buckets, arrived at by subtracting the 6 fills in the agent's head. As in session 56, the triage is plausible; as in session 56, **the `exact_match` issues went unfixed and unmentioned** (3 here, 6 there) — the severity `autoFix` binds without ambiguity. Two sessions, same file, same closing move: the guidance in this entry has not been absorbed anywhere it would take effect. Cost is one call. Consider promoting it from tracker prose into the `figma-guidelines` skill's build-completion checklist.

### [BUG-041] `fig.loadFont` reports success for a font it never loaded

- **Status**: implemented — `fig.loadFont` is now `loadFontStrict` (`helpers.js`): same resolution as `loadFontWithFallback`, but a fallback fails with the remote-font remedy. Internal callers keep the silent fallback. Test in `tests/stdlib.test.ts`.
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: plugin-bug
- **First seen**: Session 57 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 57
- **Estimated savings**: ~3 calls per custom-font script session; moves an atomic rollback from line 58 of a script to line 1
- **Description**: `fig.loadFont` is `loadFontWithFallback` (`src/figma_plugin/src/remote_entries/stdlib.js:25`), and that helper (`src/figma_plugin/src/helpers.js`) catches `loadFontAsync`'s throw, silently loads `Inter/Regular` instead, and **returns `{family: "Inter", style: "Regular"}` as a success**. Session 57 #20 opened a ~120-line scene build with `await fig.loadFont('PP Neue Montreal', 'Regular')` and `…'Semi Bold'`; both resolved. Thirty lines later the first `t.fontName = {family: 'PP Neue Montreal', …}` threw `in set_fontName: Cannot use unloaded font "PP Neue Montreal Regular". Please call figma.loadFontAsync(…) and await the returned promise first.` — prescribing exactly the call that had just silently failed. The script was atomic, so the whole scene was discarded.
- **Distinct from [BUG-033], and it is the half that entry's fix misses**: [BUG-033] proposes that "when `loadFontAsync` throws, check `listAvailableFontsAsync` and `fail()` with the true cause." In this code path `loadFontAsync`'s throw is **caught and swallowed** — it never reaches a caller that could re-report it. [BUG-033] is about font *absence*; this is about the stdlib wrapper *claiming success* for a substitution the caller never asked for and cannot see.
- **The fallback is correct for internal callers**: `setcharacters.js`, `create.js` and `apply.js` want graceful degradation — a text write should not hard-fail because a face is missing. A `run_script` author who names a family explicitly wants the opposite.
- **Proposed fix**: leave `loadFontWithFallback` unchanged; wrap it at the stdlib boundary so `fig.loadFont` compares the returned family to the requested one and `fail()`s on a mismatch — stating that the headless `use_figma` VM has no access to locally-installed or licensed fonts, and pointing at the style-face swap ([BUG-037]) as the remedy. Two-line change, no internal caller affected.
- **Note**: not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist (not sync-to-async / type-coercion / description-only / lint-scope-filter / boundary-guard / assertion) — no auto-plan generated, though the change is narrow and mechanical. Related: [BUG-033] (the underlying font absence, 5th recurrence), [BUG-035], [BUG-037], [BUG-032] (same shape: an error that states a fix for a problem that is not the one that occurred).

### [BUG-042] `lint` counts an invisible paint as an unbound fill

- **Status**: implemented — `checkColorProperty` (`lint.js`) skips a paint with `visible === false`; fills and strokes share the function. Test in `tests/minilint.test.ts`.
- **Priority**: P2
- **Category**: plugin-bug
- **First seen**: Session 57 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 57 (measured); 54 (same invisible-paint shape, folded into [TOOL-038]'s compounding-trap note)
- **Estimated savings**: ~2 calls per SVG-importing session, and it removes a class of finding that cannot be resolved by binding
- **Description**: `checkColorProperty` (`src/figma_plugin/src/commands/lint.js:399-422`) reads `paints[0]` and guards on two conditions — the paint must be `SOLID` (`:412`) and must not already carry `boundVariables.color` (`:415`) — but **never checks `paint.visible`**. `figma.createNodeFromSvg` leaves a `{type: "SOLID", visible: false, color: {r:1,g:1,b:1}}` paint on the wrapper frame it creates, so every imported SVG contributes a phantom unbound `#ffffff` fill. Session 57 #29 returned `totalIssues: 20` with `fills: 6` — **6 of 20, all six the `Indicator` wrapper frames**. #30 read one back (`fills: [{visible: false, …}]`) and #31 cleared `fills = []` on all six. Node-level visibility *is* handled (`lint.js:150` skips invisible nodes); paint-level visibility is not.
- **The finding is unactionable as reported**: a hidden paint cannot be fixed by binding a variable to it, so the only response is deletion — which is what the agent did, on a paint that was never rendering.
