- **Proposed fix**: one guard beside the existing SOLID check — `if (prop(paint, "visible") === false) return null;` — applied to both the fills and strokes paths of `checkColorProperty`. Use `prop()` rather than a direct read, per the serializer-boundary rule.
- **Note**: false-positive guard, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [TOOL-038] (the same `visible: false` paint shape, there as a *binding* trap rather than a lint one), [TOOL-043], [AGENT-034] (lint noise makes the closing triage harder to trust).

### [BUG-043] A variable-bound paint keeps its literal colour, and the remote VM renders the literal — `edit` seeds black

- **Status**: identified
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: always write `resolveForConsumer(node).value` into the paint before `setBoundVariableForPaint` — on empty and existing paints; fall back to current behaviour if resolve throws or is non-COLOR. Recorded on [#177](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/177).
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: plugin-bug
- **Auto-fixable**: no (behaviour change in the bind path)
- **First seen**: Session 58 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 58
- **Estimated savings**: ~6 calls per session that binds colour variables on new nodes; converts a silently-wrong render into a correct one
- **Description**: `figma.variables.setBoundVariableForPaint(paint, "color", variable)` attaches the binding but **does not update the paint's literal `color`**, and on the remote render path the literal is what gets drawn. Session 58 built 60 Button variants whose fills and strokes were all created as `setBoundVariableForPaint({type:'SOLID', color:{r:0,g:0,b:0}, opacity:1}, 'color', v)`. Every button rendered **black**. Script #12 read the paint back and confirmed the binding was present and correct (`boundVariables.color = {type:'VARIABLE_ALIAS', id:'VariableID:2:167'}`) while `color` was still `{0,0,0}`. Script #13 ruled out mode resolution — `explicitVariableModes` was `{}` on the set, the component, both pages and a known-good comparison component — and showed `v.resolveForConsumer(node)` returning the correct `{r:0.878, g:0.325, b:0}`. Script #14 wrote `resolveForConsumer(node).value` into the r/g/b of all ~120 bound paints alongside the binding, and the render came good.
- **Figmagent ships the same pattern, and one instance actively seeds black**:
  - `src/figma_plugin/src/commands/apply.js:266-273` — when the node has **no** fills/strokes, `edit` seeds `[{type:'SOLID', color:{r:0,g:0,b:0}, opacity:1}]` and binds onto it. `write` creates FRAME/RECTANGLE/COMPONENT nodes with empty fills by default (documented in CLAUDE.md), so `write` → `edit({variables:{fillColor}})` is the ordinary path into this bug. When paints *do* exist the prior literal survives, which is stale by construction whenever the bound variable holds a different value.
  - `src/figma_plugin/src/commands/styles.js:805-809` — `bindVariablesToStyle` seeds the identical black for a PAINT style with no paints.
  - `src/figma_plugin/src/commands/lint.js:522-526` is **safe**: it returns early on an empty paint array, and the literal it binds onto is the value lint matched, so it already equals the resolved variable. No change needed there.
- **Why writing the resolved literal is safe**: in the Figma client the binding takes precedence and the literal is only the fallback, so writing the resolved value cannot break mode switching — it only makes the fallback correct instead of black.
- **Proposed fix**: in `apply.js` (`bindVariableToNode`, the `fills`/`strokes` branch) and `styles.js` (`bindVariablesToStyle`, the PAINT branch), call `variable.resolveForConsumer(node).value` and write `r`/`g`/`b` (and `a` → `opacity`) into the paint **before** `setBoundVariableForPaint`. Fall back to the existing behaviour if `resolveForConsumer` throws or returns a non-COLOR type.
- **Note**: not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist (sync-to-async / type-coercion / description-only / lint-scope-filter / boundary-guard / assertion) — no auto-plan generated, but the change is small and localised to two functions. Related: [BUG-034] (a `fontWeight` binding that is bound but unresolved), [BUG-038] (a bound `opacity` that resolves at 1/100 scale) — three variants of "the binding exists and the rendered value is wrong".

### [TOOL-045] No way to ask what fonts the remote VM can actually render

- **Status**: identified
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: `get_design_system({ includeFonts: true, fontPattern })` → `fonts: { families, missing[] }`; no separate list_fonts tool; parking uses it for donor choice. Recorded on [#178](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/178).
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: missing-tool
- **Auto-fixable**: no (missing-tool: new includeFonts section)
- **First seen**: Session 58 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport) — as a first-class cost; the underlying hunt has recurred since session 53
- **Sessions affected**: 53, 54, 55, 56, 57, 58
- **Estimated savings**: ~5 calls per custom-font session; gives [BUG-033], [BUG-040] and [BUG-041] a place to point
- **Description**: Because [BUG-033] forces a donor-font swap on every write to a custom-font file, each session must first discover a donor the VM can render — and there is no tool for it. Session 58 spent three `run_script` calls and two `screenshot` calls on the hunt: #3 dumped `listAvailableFontsAsync()` (1,938 families) and filtered for a one-word `Semibold`; #7 swapped to `Inter / Semi Bold` and measured `w: 46`; #8 swapped to `Noto Sans New Tai Lue / Semibold` and measured `w: 51`. Sessions 53-57 each ran a variant of the same hunt.
- **The data already exists server-side**: `get_design_system` calls `figma.listAvailableFontsAsync()` at `src/figma_plugin/src/commands/styles.js:44` and again at `:709`, and discards the list both times.
- **The inventory is not stable between sessions**: sessions 53 and 58 both reported **1,938** families; session 55 reported **8,927**. An agent cannot cache a donor across sessions and must re-verify, which makes the missing tool cost recurring rather than one-off.
- **Proposed fix**: expose the inventory. Either (a) an `includeFonts: true` section on `get_design_system` returning family → styles, plus a `missing` list naming families the file's text styles and `font/family/*` variables reference that the VM does not have; or (b) a small `list_fonts({ familyPattern, style })` tool. (a) is cheaper — the call already fetches the list — and puts the answer next to the design-system read an agent makes anyway. Either form should carry the [BUG-040] caveat that presence in the list does not guarantee usable metrics.
- **Note**: new tool surface, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [BUG-033] (why a donor is needed at all), [BUG-040] (why the listed donor may still be unusable), [BUG-035] (the face-spelling hazard the list would make visible), [BUG-041] (`fig.loadFont` hiding the substitution).

### [AGENT-035] `grep({variableId: [...]})` already does the cross-page token scan agents hand-roll in `run_script`

- **Status**: implemented — `grep`'s `variableId` description now states the array + paint-level (fills/strokes) coverage and the `scope: "DOCUMENT"` idiom.
- **Priority**: P2
- **Category**: agent-behavior
- **First seen**: Session 58 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 58
- **Estimated savings**: ~1 call per reconciliation pass, and one less reason to stay inside `run_script`
- **Description**: Session 58 #18 is a 1,352-character script that walks every page, reads `node.boundVariables`, then separately walks `fills[].boundVariables.color` and `strokes[].boundVariables.color`, matching against a 37-element set of `button/*` variable IDs — to answer "which nodes outside this page still use these tokens". `grep` already does exactly this: `src/figmagent_mcp/tools/find.ts:47` declares `variableId` as a **string list**, and `src/figma_plugin/src/commands/find.js:28-75` implements all three lookups the script reimplemented, with the same fills/strokes paint-level coverage, and additionally returns ancestry paths the script did not compute. The agent had `grep` loaded and had used it once earlier in the session.
- **Why it matters beyond one call**: this is [AGENT-025]'s drift mechanism at single-call resolution. The gap is discoverability, not capability — once a session is operating inside `run_script`, the next capability question gets answered in JavaScript rather than by re-reading the tool surface.
- **Proposed fix**: one sentence in the `grep` tool description — *"`variableId` accepts an array and matches paint-level bindings on `fills`/`strokes`, so a single call answers 'which nodes anywhere use any of these tokens'."* Optionally add the same line to CLAUDE.md's design-tokens workflow bullet, where token reconciliation is described but this capability is not.
- **Note**: description/guidance change, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [AGENT-025] (the monoculture this feeds), [AGENT-030] (the other `grep`-discoverability entry), [TOOL-033].

### [BUG-044] Assigning `style.fontName` silently discards the style's `fontWeight` variable binding — the recommended font swap corrupts every consumer

- **Status**: identified
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: park-and-restore owns snapshot/restore incl. the double fontName assign; `update_styles` restores a `fontWeight` binding a `fontName` write would drop. See #145. Recorded on [#180](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/180).
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: plugin-bug
- **Auto-fixable**: no (covered by BUG-033's park-and-restore)
- **First seen**: Session 59 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 59, 61 (**remedy verified**)
- **Estimated savings**: ~2 calls per style-swap, and it removes a file-wide corruption risk from the workaround this tracker recommends
- **Description**: [BUG-037]'s style-level swap — park the style's own `fontName` on a VM-available family, apply the style, re-bind `fontFamily` last — is the remedy this tracker endorses, is 4-for-4 on applying the style, and **is lossy**. Session 59 #15 ran it verbatim on `support/label/1`:

  ```js
  style.setBoundVariable("fontFamily", null);
  style.fontName = { family: "Inter", style: "Regular" };
  // …apply to nodes…
  style.setBoundVariable("fontFamily", sansVar);
  ```

  The style went in on `PP Neue Montreal / Semibold` with `boundVariables` `{fontSize, fontFamily, fontWeight}` and came out on `PP Neue Montreal / **Regular**` with `styleBound: ["fontFamily", "fontSize"]`. **Assigning `fontName` drops an existing `fontWeight` binding, and the face does not come back with the family re-bind.** On a design-system file the blast radius is every component on that style — here `Accordion` and the rest of the Archer file, none of which the session touched.
- **The repair has its own trap** (#17): re-binding `fontWeight` *rewrites the face literal*, so `fontName` must be assigned **again after** the weight bind, or the restore silently no-ops:

  ```js
  style.fontName = face;
  style.setBoundVariable("fontWeight", weightVar);
  style.fontName = face; // re-set: the weight lookup rewrites the face literal
  style.lineHeight = { unit: "PERCENT", value: 120.00000476837158 };
  style.setBoundVariable("fontFamily", sansVar);
  ```

  Restoring `lineHeight` explicitly was also required. Cost: 1 script to detect (#16), 1 to repair (#17) — 2 of the session's 4 scripts existed only to undo damage the first one did.
- **A concurrent writer is ruled out**: sessions 54 and 55 were live on this file when session 59 opened, but 54's last call is 23:16:17, 55's is 23:16:29 → 23:24:52, and the destructive write is 23:20:11 — no other process touched the file in that window. The loss is the script's own.
- **Why it slipped past everything**: `run_script` returned `is_error: false` on the destructive call and carries no post-write assertions ([TOOL-033]). The loss was visible **only** because the agent's own `return` block echoed `styleBound` and `styleFont`. A script returning `{ok: true}` leaves the file damaged and the session reporting success. Session 57 restored `fontWeight`/`lineHeight`/`letterSpacing` explicitly and so never saw this; session 59 followed the shorter recipe as documented and did.
- **Proposed fix**: (a) whatever surface performs the swap must snapshot `fontName` + `boundVariables` before parking and restore both after — that is the concrete content of [BUG-037] fix (b), *have `edit` do the swap internally*, and the reason it should not be left to script authors; (b) if `update_styles` writes `fontName` while a `fontWeight` binding exists, warn or re-bind rather than dropping it silently; (c) correct the swap recipe wherever it is written down ([BUG-033], [BUG-037], the `edit` description, CLAUDE.md's *"Custom fonts are absent on the remote transport"* paragraph) to include the weight/face/lineHeight restore and the double `fontName` assignment.
- **Note**: plugin/API-semantics bug plus a documentation correction — not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist, no auto-plan generated. Related: [BUG-037] (the recipe this corrects), [BUG-033] (the font absence underneath), [BUG-034] (inert weight bindings), [TOOL-037] (`edit` cannot bind `fontWeight` at all), [TOOL-033] (the missing assertions), [AGENT-032].

- **Remedy verified in production (session 61)** — script #27 applies the prescribed double-assign verbatim: `t.fontName = face; t.setBoundVariable('fontWeight', SEMI); t.fontName = face;`. The style kept its face, the weight binding held, and no consumer was corrupted. Sessions 53–59 lost scripts, deliverables and whole builds to the font failure class ([BUG-033], [BUG-040], this entry); **session 61 lost nothing, at the cost of one 11K-char Bash read of the project memory file**. That is the clearest end-to-end evidence in the tracker that written-down findings pay for themselves.

### [TOOL-046] `write`/`edit` cannot set an IMAGE paint — every image-bearing component ships as a placeholder disc

- **Status**: identified
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: probe `createImageAsync(url)` on the remote VM first; if it works add `fillImage: { url, scaleMode }` to write/edit, else document the limitation next to fillColor and close. Recorded on [#181](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/181).
- **Priority**: P2
- **Category**: missing-tool
- **Auto-fixable**: no (pending the createImageAsync probe)
- **First seen**: Session 59 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 59
- **Estimated savings**: 0 calls — this is a fidelity gap, not a cost one
- **Description**: `imageHash` appears **nowhere** in `src/figma_plugin/src/` or `src/figmagent_mcp/tools/`; `createImage` appears only in `run_script`'s guard list (`src/figmagent_mcp/tools/script.ts:59`). There is no way to attach an IMAGE paint to a node through any first-class tool. Session 59's `Avatar` has a `Content=Image` variant whose entire point is an image fill; it shipped as a flat `color/neutral/300` disc, annotated as a placeholder. Every avatar, thumbnail, card media, hero and empty-state illustration in every mirrored component has the same problem.
- **Why it is not simply "use `run_script`"**: `figma.createImage` needs bytes, and the headless VM has no obvious route to them — so unlike most gaps in this tracker, the script escape hatch does not clearly close this one either. That is worth establishing before designing the tool.
- **Proposed fix**: investigate whether the remote VM can reach `figma.createImageAsync(url)`; if it can, expose `fillImage: { url }` (and `scaleMode`) on `write`/`edit`, matching the existing `fillColor` shape. If it cannot, say so in the `write` description next to the `fillColor` note, so the limitation is discovered at authoring time rather than at annotation time.
- **Note**: missing-tool capability gap — not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist, no auto-plan generated. Related: [TOOL-032] (`write` cannot bind variables at create time — same "the paint family is under-served" shape), [TOOL-039].

### [TOOL-047] `combine_as_variants` cannot name the set it creates, and reports nothing about what its auto-layout did to the variants

- **Status**: planned
- **Partially shipped**: (a) `combine_as_variants` accepts an optional `name` and sets it on the resulting set. (b) per-child sizing readback / assertions over re-parented children still open.
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: (b) after combining, run the existing post-write assertions over the re-parented children and return `warnings:` plus each child's sizing/width/height. (a) `name` shipped in #194. Recorded on [#182](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/182).
- **Priority**: P2
- **Category**: missing-tool
- **Auto-fixable**: yes (assertion)
- **First seen**: Session 59 (2026-09-01, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 59
- **Estimated savings**: ~2-3 calls per component set
- **Description**: Two small defects in one call. **(a) No `name`**: the tool takes `componentIds` and `parentId` and nothing else (`src/figmagent_mcp/tools/components.ts`), so session 59 #18 produced a COMPONENT_SET named **"Component 1"** and #19 existed to rename it. Every component set built with this tool pays a rename. **(b) No sizing readback**: the call applies auto-layout to the set it creates — its own description says *"horizontal wrap auto-layout (20px spacing, 40px padding, HUG sizing)"* — and returns only `{id, name, type, childCount, children:[{id,name,type}]}`. Session 59 then screenshotted (#24), re-asserted `layoutSizingHorizontal/Vertical: "FIXED"` with explicit 32/40/48 dimensions on all six variants (#25), and screenshotted again (#26). The transcript carries no thinking blocks, so the trigger for #25 is inferred from the sequence; the repair itself is explicit in the payload.
- **The general shape**: `write`/`edit`'s post-write assertions (balloon frame, width collapse, FILL-not-applied) inspect the node that was *written*. They do not inspect siblings that a layout change *re-flowed* — which is precisely what wrapping N components in an auto-layout set does.
- **Proposed fix**: (a) add an optional `name` to `combine_as_variants` and set it on the resulting set; (b) return each child's post-wrap `layoutSizingHorizontal`/`layoutSizingVertical`/`width`/`height`, or run the existing assertions over the children it just re-parented and emit a `warnings:` block. (b) generalises: any operation that gives a frame auto-layout should assert over its children, not itself.
- **Note**: missing-tool/response-shape gap — not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist, no auto-plan generated. Related: [BUG-036] (the other `combine_as_variants` entry), [AGENT-028] (auto-layout silently flipping sizing), [TOOL-033].

### [BUG-045] `get_reactions` silently drops every `CHANGE_TO` reaction — an interactive component always reads as "no interactions"

- **Status**: implemented — the `CHANGE_TO` filter is gone from `getReactions` (`document.js`); every reaction is returned. The `reaction_to_connector_strategy` prompt already drops CHANGE_TO at its Step 3, the only consumer that needs to.
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: plugin-bug
- **First seen**: Session 60 (2026-09-02, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 60
- **Estimated savings**: ~1 call per verification, and retires a tool that returns a confidently wrong answer with `is_error: false`
- **Description**: Session 60 wrote 10 prototype reactions across 8 variants of the `Accordion Item` set, then called `get_reactions(["11:2","11:8","11:26","11:34","11:14","11:42"])` to verify. Those six nodes held **10 reactions** (2/2/2/2/1/1, per the write script's own readback). The tool returned `{"nodesCount":6,"nodesWithReactions":0,"nodes":[]}`. A `run_script` seven seconds later read `n.reactions` on the same ids and got every one back with full trigger/destination/transition/duration detail — so this is not a stale-read or transport artifact.
- **Root cause (confirmed by code read, not inference)**: `src/figma_plugin/src/commands/document.js:67-73` filters the reaction list before returning it: `if (r.action && r.action.navigation === "CHANGE_TO") return false;` and `if (Array.isArray(r.actions)) return !r.actions.some(a => a.navigation === "CHANGE_TO");`. **Every variant-swap reaction in an interactive component is `CHANGE_TO`**, so for the entire class of node an agent most wants to audit, the tool's answer is structurally always "none". The filter belongs to the FigJam connector-drawing use case — a same-frame variant swap has no meaningful connector line — but it is applied in the *read*, so the data never reaches the caller at all.
- **Why the blast radius is bigger than one call**: session 60 had just written the reactions and knew the answer was wrong. An agent auditing an unfamiliar file's prototype wiring would conclude the file has no interactions and act on it — and nothing in the response signals that anything was filtered (`nodesCount: 6` implies six nodes were examined and found bare).
- **Proposed fix**: remove the filter from `getReactions` and return every reaction; drop `CHANGE_TO` inside `reaction_to_connector_strategy`, where the connector params are actually built, which is the only consumer that needs it. If a filtered form is still wanted, make it an explicit opt-in parameter defaulting to *include*. Also report a `filtered` count if anything is ever withheld, so an empty result is never indistinguishable from a suppressed one.
- **Note**: 6-line filter removal + prompt change; not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist (not sync-to-async / type-coercion / description-only / lint-scope-filter / boundary-guard / assertion), no auto-plan generated. Related: [TOOL-048] (the missing writer this read exists to verify), [BUG-046] (the other `get_reactions` defect).

### [TOOL-048] No tool authors prototype reactions — interactive components are `run_script`-only

- **Status**: identified
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: `reactions` field on `edit`, compact form expanded in the plugin to Figma's Reaction[] via setReactionsAsync; `[]` clears; `raw` escape for anything the compact form lacks. Recorded on [#184](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/184).
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: missing-tool
- **Auto-fixable**: no (missing-tool: reactions on edit)
- **First seen**: Session 60 (2026-09-02, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 60
- **Estimated savings**: 1 script → 1 batched call per interactive component; removes an entire category of work from the escape hatch
- **Description**: Figmagent's prototyping surface reads and draws but never writes. `get_reactions` reads (badly — see [BUG-045]), `create_connections` draws FigJam connector lines, `set_default_connector` configures those lines; nothing calls `setReactionsAsync`. `grep -rn "setReactionsAsync" src/` returns **zero** hits. Session 60's entire deliverable — hover/click behaviour on an 8-variant `Accordion Item` set, timed to the component's real CSS transition tokens — went through a single 1,575-character `run_script`.
- **The script is almost all boilerplate**: three factory helpers (`anim`, `toVariant`, `click`/`enter`/`leave` wrapping `{type:'NODE', navigation:'CHANGE_TO', transition:{type:'SMART_ANIMATE', easing:{type:'CUSTOM_CUBIC_BEZIER', easingFunctionCubicBezier:{x1,y1,x2,y2}}, duration}}`), an 8-entry variant-id map, an 8-row plan table, then `for (const [id, reactions] of plan) await (await figma.getNodeByIdAsync(id)).setReactionsAsync(reactions)`. The actual payload is 10 reactions expressible in ~10 lines. Every session that wires an interactive component re-derives the same navigation/easing shape.
- **Why it batches cleanly**: `setReactionsAsync(reactions)` is a plain async whole-array setter keyed by node — the exact shape `edit` already uses. `reactions: []` clears, which is what session 60's two Disabled variants needed (deliberately inert, matching `pointer-events: none` in the SCSS).
- **Proposed fix**: `set_reactions({ nodes: [{ nodeId, reactions: [...] }] })`, or a `reactions` field on `edit`. Accept a compact authoring form — `{ on: "click" | "mouseEnter" | "mouseLeave" | "hover", to: nodeId, navigation: "CHANGE_TO" | "NAVIGATE" | "BACK", transition: "SMART_ANIMATE" | "DISSOLVE" | "INSTANT", duration, easing }` — and expand to Figma's verbose object server-side, mirroring how `edit`'s `variables` field hides `setBoundVariable`. Ship with [BUG-045]: a writer whose only verification read returns `[]` is worse than no writer.
- **Note**: new tool surface, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [BUG-045], [BUG-046], [AGENT-025] (this is a *justified* `run_script`, the counter-example the monoculture entry needs).

### [BUG-046] `get_reactions` appends an unconditional "You MUST … required next step" — false on an empty result, and false for the read case

- **Status**: implemented — `get_reactions` is described as a read; the connector hint is appended only when `nodesWithReactions > 0` and phrased as optional (`buildReactionsResult` in `tools/scan.ts`). Test in `tests/no-op-verdicts.test.ts`.
- **Priority**: P2
- **Category**: plugin-bug
- **First seen**: Session 60 (2026-09-02, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 60
- **Estimated savings**: ~0 calls; removes a false instruction that mis-steers agents toward FigJam output on a design file
- **Description**: `src/figmagent_mcp/tools/scan.ts:12` describes the tool as *"CRITICAL: The output MUST be processed using the 'reaction_to_connector_strategy' prompt IMMEDIATELY to generate parameters for connector lines via the 'create_connections' tool"*, and `:27` appends a hardcoded second content block: *"IMPORTANT: You MUST now use the reaction data above and follow the `reaction_to_connector_strategy` prompt … This is a required next step."* Session 60's call returned **zero** nodes and the agent's purpose was verifying a write it had just made; the instruction was false on both counts. The agent correctly ignored it — but "correctly ignore the tool's own stated requirement" is not a property to rely on.
- **Root cause**: upstream cruft. `get_reactions` was step 1 of a two-step FigJam pipeline and never got re-scoped once it became the natural verification read for prototype work.
- **Proposed fix**: re-describe as *"read prototype reactions from one or more nodes"*; emit the connector suggestion only when `nodesWithReactions > 0`, and phrase it as an option (*"to visualise these as FigJam connectors, see `reaction_to_connector_strategy`"*), not a requirement. Keep the `followUp` prompt hint, drop the imperative text block.
- **Note**: description/response-shape change; not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [BUG-045] (same tool, the substantive defect), [TOOL-011] (the other "tool description states something false" entry).

### [BUG-047] Concurrent remote sessions share `figma.currentPage` — a parentless `write` lands on another session's page

- **Status**: identified
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: per-session working page: `use_file({ page })` / URL node-id sets it, `set_focus` updates it on remote, every create handler sets it before creating; no working page + no parentId fails with the fix. Serial sessions on one file until this lands. Recorded on [#186](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/186).
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: plugin-bug
- **Auto-fixable**: no (design work: per-session working page)
- **First seen**: Session 61 (2026-09-02, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 61
- **Estimated savings**: ~2 calls per collision, and removes a silent cross-session corruption channel
- **Description**: **The first proven cross-session write collision in this tracker.** Session 61 (`4ac78b46`, building `Context Menu` on page `5:685`) and session 60 (`e95d2d76`, building `Accordion` on page `11:58`) ran concurrently on file `C4zLeQJs8qkAhFSLwMKP9J`. Session 61's script #27 called `setCurrentPageAsync(5:685)` at **03:35:12**. Thirty-five seconds later session 60 called `write({node:{type:"FRAME", name:"probe-frame", x:0, y:2000, width:560, height:200}})` with **no `parentId`** — and the frame (`63:322`) landed on session 61's `Context Menu` page, not on the Accordion page session 60 was working in. Session 61's `read({nodeId:"5:685", detail:"layout", depth:1})` at **03:36:15** duly reported a foreign `probe-frame` in its own page listing; session 60 deleted it at **03:36:18**; session 61's cleanup `edit({nodeId:"63:322", delete:true})` at **03:36:26** failed with `Node not found`. Every timestamp is in the two transcripts.
- **Why this is new**: [BUG-018] establishes that `currentPage` **does not persist between remote calls within a session**. This adds the other half — it is also **not private to a session**. Every connected MCP process reads and writes one shared page pointer on the file, so a parentless create is non-deterministic whenever more than one process is live. Session 59's analysis explicitly ruled out a concurrent writer by timestamp; this one proves one.
- **Blast radius**: any `write` without `parentId`, any raw `clone()` ([AGENT-033]), any `createFrame()`/`createComponent()` in a script that relies on default parenting, and `combineAsVariants`' page co-location check ([BUG-036]). All of them resolve against a pointer another process can move mid-build.
- **Proposed fix**: (a) in `create.js`, when no `parentId` is given, resolve the target page from the calling session's own file/page context and `setCurrentPageAsync` to it **inside the handler on every call** — the identical remedy [BUG-018] already verified 3/3 for the import path; (b) state in `run_script`'s description that `setCurrentPageAsync` mutates state other processes observe, so a script must set it itself and must not assume it is still set; (c) consider having `read` mark nodes whose `lastModified` postdates the session's own first write, so a foreign artifact is visible as foreign rather than as the agent's own mess.
- **Message quality — a positive note**: the failure text (*"Node not found: 63:322. Fix: verify the ID with read or search with grep — it may have been deleted or belong to another page"*) named the true cause. The node **had** been deleted. On an entry family whose usual complaint is a fix that states the wrong problem ([BUG-025], [BUG-032], [BUG-039]), this one was right.
- **Note**: handler + description change, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [BUG-018] (same shared pointer, intra-session half), [AGENT-033], [BUG-036], [TOOL-023].

### [TOOL-049] `lint` counts COMPONENT_SET wrapper chrome as unbound-token issues — 67% of findings were unactionable

- **Status**: implemented — `lint.js` skips a COMPONENT_SET node's own `cornerRadius`/`itemSpacing`/`counterAxisSpacing`/`padding*`; children are linted unchanged. Test in `tests/minilint.test.ts`.
- **Priority**: P2
- **Category**: plugin-bug
- **First seen**: Session 61 (2026-09-02, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 61 (measured); 56 (asserted without measurement, see [TOOL-036])
- **Estimated savings**: ~0 calls; removes the recurring need to hand-triage a lint result in prose, and removes the cover a real finding can hide under
- **Description**: Session 61 #31 ran `lint({nodeId:["62:73","63:75","63:2","63:4","63:272"], maxIssues: 60})` over 94 nodes and returned 21 issues. **Fourteen of them — exactly seven per COMPONENT_SET, on both sets — are the set wrapper's own auto-layout**: `cornerRadius=5`, `itemSpacing=40`, `counterAxisSpacing=40`, `paddingTop/Right/Bottom/Left=40` on `62:73` and identically on `63:75`. The `cornerRadius: 5` is Figma's own default on the dashed wrapper `combineAsVariants` creates; the `40`s are canvas gutter between variants, set deliberately by the build script as page presentation. Neither is part of the component's design contract and neither has — or should have — a matching token. The remaining 7 are `opacity: 0.4000000059604645` literals, which are [BUG-038]'s workaround debt, so **the actionable count for this build was zero**.
- **Why it matters beyond noise**: [TOOL-036] flags session 56 for closing a build on the claim that its 93 lint issues were "set-wrapper chrome and deliberate literal opacities" — a triage made from summary buckets with 33 issues never returned. Session 61 measures the same claim on a full, uncapped issue list and finds it exactly true. The claim is correct *and* it is the reason a genuine finding could pass unnoticed inside it.
- **Proposed fix**: in `lint.js`, skip a node's own `padding*` / `itemSpacing` / `counterAxisSpacing` / `cornerRadius` when `node.type === "COMPONENT_SET"` (continue into its children unchanged). Or report them under a separate `scaffolding` bucket that `autoFix` never touches and the summary counts separately. Either turns this session's 21-issue result into a 7-issue result that is entirely about the design.
- **Note**: narrow filter change in one file with a clear predicate; a candidate for an auto-fix plan if the allowlist is extended to lint-scope filters. Related: [TOOL-036] (the triage-from-summary defect this measures), [BUG-042] (the other "lint counts something unbindable" entry), [BUG-038], [TOOL-047].

### [TOOL-050] `create_styles` implements the variable-bound text-style recipe correctly — and its description sends agents to `run_script` instead

- **Status**: implemented — `create_styles`/`update_styles` descriptions list `fontWeight`, carry a worked remote example (author on Inter, bind the family through `variables.fontFamily`), and `loadFontOrFail`'s fix names font absence alongside spelling. The end-to-end `create_styles` run on a custom-font file is still unverified in a session.
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: missing-tool
- **First seen**: Session 62 (2026-09-02, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 62
- **Estimated savings**: ~5 calls/session on any custom-font file, and retires a description that understates the tool against the field it is most needed for
- **Description**: Session 62 needed 8 `ui/*` text styles with `fontFamily`/`fontWeight`/`fontSize` variable-bound, on a file whose family (`PP Neue Montreal`) the remote VM cannot load. It solved it from scratch in five `run_script` calls — #6 (bound fonts first, then errored on the `lineHeight` write), #7 (probed binding `lineHeight`/`letterSpacing`; they bind but force unit PIXELS, so `1.5` renders `1.5px` — rejected), #8 (rebuilt in the correct order), #9 (applied it to the other seven), #10 (read back). **`create_styles` already does exactly what #8 discovered.** `createStyles` (`src/figma_plugin/src/commands/styles.js:903-957`) runs `createTextStyle()` → load+set font (`:909-910`) → `fontSize`/`lineHeight`/`letterSpacing` (`:912-920`) → `bindVariablesToStyle` **last** (`:957`). One call with `fontFamily: "Inter", fontStyle: "Regular"` plus `variables: { fontFamily, fontWeight, fontSize }` reproduces the verified sequence for all eight.
- **Two description defects block the discovery**: (1) *"TEXT styles require valid fontFamily+fontStyle — fonts are loaded automatically"* (`src/figmagent_mcp/tools/tokens.ts:661`) reads as *name the real family*; doing so throws at `loadFontOrFail` (`styles.js:8-17`), whose stated fix — *"pass a fontFamily/fontStyle pair that exists exactly as Figma lists it"* — describes a **spelling** problem when the actual problem is font **absence**. Nothing anywhere says *author on a loadable face and bind the family variable*. (2) The `variables` field lists seven bindable TEXT properties and **omits `fontWeight`** (`tokens.ts:723`), even though `bindVariablesToStyle` (`styles.js:811-815`) passes any non-`color` field straight to `style.setBoundVariable` — so `fontWeight` works on this surface **today**, unlike on `edit`/`fig.bindVariable` ([TOOL-037]).
- **Root cause**: documentation, not capability. The implementation is correct; the session re-derived it empirically at a cost of 5 calls.
- **Proposed fix**: add `fontWeight` to the `variables` list in the `create_styles` and `update_styles` descriptions; add a worked example for a fully variable-bound text style on an unavailable family; make `loadFontOrFail` consult `listAvailableFontsAsync` and, when the *family* is absent, `fail()` with the bind-the-family remedy rather than the spelling one.
- **Caveat on the reading**: this is a code-path reading plus the session's own step-by-step empirical proof of each element (#6 proved binding-family-first breaks metric writes; #8 proved metrics-then-bindings works; `styles.js` shows create_styles uses that order). It has **not** been executed as a single `create_styles` call end-to-end. One latent fragility to check when it is: `bindVariablesToStyle` iterates `Object.keys(variables)` with no ordering guarantee, so a caller-supplied key order is what decides whether `fontFamily` binds before or after `fontWeight` — harmless in the observed paths (all are `setBoundVariable`, none load a font) but unpinned.
- **Note**: description + error-message fix, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Sharpens [TOOL-037] by locating one surface where `fontWeight` binding already works. Related: [TOOL-037], [BUG-033] (the misreported failure, at a call site that entry does not name), [AGENT-032] (which tells agents to use text styles — this is the tool that should make that cheap), [AGENT-025].

### [TOOL-051] `grep` can find nodes that *use* a style or variable, but not nodes that lack one

- **Status**: identified
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: typed absence criteria on grep: `unstyled: true` (no style of the kind the node type implies) and `unbound: [props]` (present, non-default, no binding — lint's predicates). No general `negate`. Recorded on [#189](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/189).
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: missing-tool
- **Auto-fixable**: no (missing-tool: new grep criteria)
- **First seen**: Session 62 (2026-09-02, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 62
- **Estimated savings**: ~4 calls/session on any token- or style-coverage audit
- **Description**: Four of session 62's 17 diagnostic `run_script` calls exist solely to answer *"which TEXT nodes have no text style"* — #14 (per-page `name :: style` inventory tallying `NONE`/`MIXED`), #15 (`page total/unstyled` rows), #19 (`totalUnstyled` after the first apply pass), #24 (`unstyledCount` as the final gate). The presence half of the same question is already a one-liner: `grep({ styleId: [...] })` (`src/figmagent_mcp/tools/find.ts:52,102`) finds nodes *using* a style, and #11/#21 duplicate that in script form. There is no negation and no `hasStyle: false`.
- **`lint` does not close the gap**: `src/figma_plugin/src/commands/lint.js` never reads `textStyleId` or `styleId` — it scans for unbound *property values*, so "a TEXT node carrying no text style at all" is invisible to it. There is currently no first-class way to ask what has **not** been done.
- **Cost shape**: this is the coverage-audit loop of any styling pass — establish a baseline, apply, re-measure, gate. Session 62 ran it four times and each run is a full `figma.root.children` × `loadAsync` × `findAllWithCriteria` document traversal written from scratch ([TOOL-031] is why none of them share a preamble).
- **Proposed fix**: add negation to `grep` — either `hasStyle: false` / `hasVariable: false` booleans alongside the existing `styleId`/`variableId` criteria, or a general `negate: true` flag that inverts whichever criterion is supplied. The `visibleOnly` filter is precedent for a boolean that changes what the traversal admits.
- **Note**: capability addition, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [AGENT-035] (the presence half `grep` already covers and agents still hand-roll), [TOOL-031], [AGENT-025].

### [AGENT-036] Tool schemas are selected once for the first phase and never re-searched when the task crosses domains

- **Status**: identified
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: skill rule (INFRA-008): under deferred schemas, ToolSearch the Figma domain before writing a run_script in it; CLAUDE.md's 'No ToolSearch needed' line scoped to this repo. Recorded on [#190](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/190).
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: agent-behavior
- **Auto-fixable**: no (covered by INFRA-008's plan)
- **First seen**: Session 62 (2026-09-02, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 62
- **Estimated savings**: ~4 calls/session; a distinct and fixable driver of [AGENT-025]
- **Description**: Session 62 ran under deferred tool schemas (external repo — Figmagent's 45 tools surfaced by name only). Both ToolSearch calls landed in the first 8 minutes: 03:22 `select:use_file, get_design_system, update_variables, read, create_variables` and 03:30 `select:run_script`. The **text-style phase began at 03:41** and ran 43 minutes. `create_styles` and `update_styles` were never loaded, so they were never candidates — and `update_styles` supports `delete: true` in batch (`tokens.ts:766-771`), which is exactly what `run_script` #13 hand-rolled to delete two stale styles, while `create_styles` covers #6-#9 ([TOOL-050]).
- **Why this is not [AGENT-025] restated**: the same session shows the *other* cause operating independently. `update_variables` and `create_variables` **were** loaded at 03:22 and still lost to `run_script` for #1 (596 scope updates + orphan delete) and #25 (2 aliased variables with scopes) — see [TOOL-052]. So one phase failed on discovery and another failed on preference, in one transcript. Fixing either alone leaves the other.
- **Contributing factor worth naming**: this project's CLAUDE.md states *"No ToolSearch needed — the MCP server instructions enumerate all 39 available tools by domain."* That is true in this repo and false in every external repo where the harness defers the schemas — which is where 10 of the last 10 analysed Figma sessions actually ran.
- **Proposed fix**: agent rule — before writing a `run_script` for work in a Figma domain whose schemas you have not fetched (styles, components, annotations, libraries, tokens), spend one ToolSearch on that domain first. Add the external-repo caveat to CLAUDE.md's "No ToolSearch needed" line so it does not read as universal.
- **Note**: agent-behavior / documentation change, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [AGENT-025] (the outcome), [TOOL-050], [TOOL-052], [AGENT-026].

### [TOOL-052] `update_variables` has no bulk property-only form — a 596-variable scope change is 48KB as a first-class call, 2.5KB as a script

- **Status**: identified
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: bulk form on update_variables `{ variableIds, scopes?, description?, hiddenFromPublishing? }` through the existing validation; create_variables takes a per-variable collection override. Recorded on [#191](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/191).
- **Priority**: P2
- **Category**: missing-tool
- **Auto-fixable**: no (missing-tool: bulk form on update_variables)
- **First seen**: Session 62 (2026-09-02, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 62
- **Estimated savings**: ~0 calls — the value is moving the session's single largest mutation back behind scope validation
- **Description**: Session 62's #1 set `scopes` on **596 variables** and deleted one orphan in a single `run_script`. `update_variables` covers both operations (`src/figmagent_mcp/tools/tokens.ts:278` `scopes`, `:283` `delete`) and is batch-first, and its schema **was loaded into context** at 03:22 — so unlike [AGENT-036] this is preference, not discovery. The visible incentive is payload shape: the script encodes all 596 as five run-length strings of numeric ID suffixes (`"8,9,10,…"` keyed by `"ALL_FILLS|STROKE_COLOR|EFFECT_COLOR"`), ~2.5KB, because `update_variables` requires one object per variable each repeating a full `VariableID:2:NNN` string — on the order of 48KB for the same change. The escape hatch is genuinely the compact path here, and it also made scope-set + orphan-delete atomic in one call.
- **What it costs**: 596 writes bypassed the scope validation `create_variables`/`update_variables` perform (`tokens.ts:171` — *"Scopes are validated before creation — invalid scopes return an error without creating the variable"*). The session compensated with its own verification (#2 tally, #3 checksum) — good practice, but it is re-implementing the guarantee the tool exists to provide.
- **Secondary instance**: #25 created two variables with per-mode aliases and inline scopes — fully expressible via `create_variables` (`tokens.ts:193-210` scopes, `:206-208` `{ alias: … }` values). The only friction is that `create_variables` takes one collection per call and the two tokens live in `Color` and `Viewport`, making it 2 calls vs 1.
- **Proposed fix**: add a property-only bulk form to `update_variables` — `{ scopes: [...], variableIds: [...] }` (and the same shape for `description`) applying one value across many ids. Optionally let `create_variables` accept a per-variable `collectionName`/`collectionId` so multi-collection creates are one call.
- **Note**: capability addition, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [AGENT-026] (`run_script` for work a first-class tool covers), [AGENT-025], [AGENT-036].

### [TOOL-053] `get_design_system` has no collections-only mode — agents reinvent an empty-regex probe

- **Status**: implemented — `get_design_system({ collectionsOnly: true })` returns just the collection names without fetching variables or styles. Test in `tests/design-system.test.ts`.
- **Priority**: P2
- **Category**: missing-tool
- **First seen**: Session 62 (2026-09-02, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 62
- **Estimated savings**: ~1 call/session
- **Description**: Session 62 #12 called `get_design_system({ namePattern: "^zzzz", includeStyles: false })` — a regex chosen to match nothing — purely to get the collection list back without the variables. It worked: `{"variables":[],"collections":["Color","Static","Viewport"]}`. The data is already produced on the truncation path (an over-budget response lists available collection names, per CLAUDE.md's design-tokens section), but there is no way to ask for it directly, so each agent invents the trick.
- **Proposed fix**: add `collectionsOnly: true` to `get_design_system`, or document the empty-pattern idiom in the tool description so it stops being rediscovered.
- **Note**: small capability + description change, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [TOOL-024], [TOOL-043].

### [TOOL-054] No document-scope override audit — `get_instance_overrides` reads one instance, for copying

- **Status**: identified
- **Decision (2026-09-02)**: get_instance_overrides takes `nodeIds` or `scope` plus `fields`, returns per-instance overridden fields with main-component resolution; report only, never offers to clear. Recorded on [#193](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/193).
- **Priority**: P2
- **Category**: missing-tool
- **Auto-fixable**: no (missing-tool: scoped override audit)
- **First seen**: Session 62 (2026-09-02, external storybook/"Archer", remote transport)
- **Sessions affected**: 62
- **Estimated savings**: ~2 calls/session on any file where instances have drifted from their mains
- **Description**: Applying a text style to a TEXT node inside an INSTANCE creates a `textStyleId` override that renders identically but will not follow if the main is later re-pointed. Session 62 needed to know how much of that it had created, and spent three scripts: #20 (count instances with a `textStyleId` override per page), #27 (re-walk the document and list them with main-component and containing-frame context — `totalInstances: 60`), #28 (classify each as safe-to-reset vs would-lose-content — `safeToReset: 0`). `get_instance_overrides` (`src/figmagent_mcp/tools/components.ts:245-252`) takes a single `nodeId` and exists to *copy* overrides to other instances; there is no scoped read and no field filter.
- **Cost shape**: #27 and #28 are two traversals of the same question, split only because #27's answer prompted the follow-up "is resetting safe" — one call returning both the inventory and the per-instance override fields would have covered it.
- **Proposed fix**: accept `nodeIds` (or a page/document scope) plus an optional `fields` filter on `get_instance_overrides`, returning per-instance overridden fields with main-component resolution. Note the related dead end the session hit and recorded to project memory: Figma offers no **per-field** override reset — `resetOverrides()` also wipes text content — so the tool should report, not offer to clear.
- **Note**: capability addition, not in the Phase 6 auto-fix allowlist — no auto-plan generated. Related: [AGENT-032] (styling mains before instances exist is the way to avoid this entirely), [TOOL-051] (the same "audit what state the document is in" gap).

### [INFRA-006] Widen the auto-fix allowlist: description/error-text, lint-scope filters, boundary guards, assertion categories — [#195](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/195)
- **Status**: implemented — allowlist widened in the `analyze-session` skill (Phase 6: `description-only`, `lint-scope-filter`, `boundary-guard`, `assertion`, each with a dispatch gate), `dispatch-fixes` (P2 allowed for the two low-risk patterns; cap 4/run, ≤2 guards/assertions; a plan naming no test aborts) and CLAUDE.md; seven plans backfilled under `.claude/plans/2026-09-02-*.md`.
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: infrastructure
- **First seen**: cohort synthesis of sessions 53–62 (2026-09-02)
- **Description**: see the decision recorded on the issue. `/dispatch-fixes` may auto-open draft PRs for four more categories, in addition to today's sync-to-async / type-coercion / missing-batch-tool:

### [INFRA-007] sync-issues: reverse-sync a closed GitHub issue to tracker Status `implemented` — [#196](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/196)
- **Status**: planned
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: infrastructure
- **Auto-fixable**: no (script change in sync-tracker-issues.ts)
- **First seen**: cohort synthesis of sessions 53–62 (2026-09-02)
- **Description**: see the decision recorded on the issue. when a linked GitHub issue is closed but the tracker's active entry still says `identified` / `planned`, `sync-issues` updates the tracker instead of only reporting DRIFT: the Status line becomes `implemented — closed on GitHub <date> (#N)`. DRIFT stays for the opposite direction (tracker resolved, issue still open), which still needs a human. Guard: never reverse-sync an entry whose Status was edited in the same run, and never reopen.

### [INFRA-008] Promote the remote-VM gotchas from the storybook repo's memory into the figma-guidelines skill — [#197](https://github.com/dabowman/Figmagent/issues/197)
- **Status**: planned
- **Priority**: P1
- **Category**: infrastructure
- **Auto-fixable**: yes (description-only)
- **First seen**: cohort synthesis of sessions 53–62 (2026-09-02)
- **Description**: see the decision recorded on the issue. the remote-VM gotchas live in the `figma-guidelines` skill (`skills/figma-guidelines/SKILL.md`) — loaded per Figma task in every repo via the plugin, zero cost otherwise. Not the MCP server instructions (token cost on every session) and not tool descriptions alone (point-of-use only).

## Issue Categories

- `missing-batch-tool` — tool exists but lacks batch variant
- `plugin-bug` — bug in Figma plugin code
- `type-coercion` — MCP server rejects valid-but-wrong-type input
- `missing-tool` — capability gap requiring new tool
- `agent-behavior` — prompt/skill improvement needed
- `infrastructure` — WebSocket, reconnection, schema freshness
